# TASKS — the queue

Live state of the loop.
Priority rule: **process blockers (`P-*`) outrank science tasks, and cheap bounds outrank expensive calculations.**
This overrides the default pull toward whatever has the best ROI.

Status vocabulary: `TODO` · `IN PROGRESS` · `BLOCKED` · `DONE` · `KILLED` (branch abandoned, with reason).

IDs: `T*` are the eight tasks of the problem definition §6, `P*` are process tasks we raised ourselves.
The `Leaf` column is the NDI `simulation-task-map` ID the work traces to.

## Start here — the state after iteration 24

Written for whoever picks the loop up next. The queue below is the authority; this is only the reading order.
**Superseding the iteration-21 note, which three iterations have overtaken.**

**Read these four first, because they move things the rest of the file still assumes.**

- **THE FLATNESS NEGATIVE IS OVERTURNED, and not by a coupling.** §3's own parameter row says
  *"Tile thickness ~10 nm (single-layer honeycomb)"*; every structural claim before iteration 23 modelled a
  **2 nm** tile. On the tile §3 actually specifies — **four** honeycomb layers, which one circular M13 pays
  for exactly (**6 720** of 7 249 nt) — the free tile dishes **0.0577199433** of the stroke under `C-0022`'s
  solved collar **with no attachment coupling at all**, inside `T-5b`'s 0.10, against the single layer's
  **0.307902368** (`C-0109`). `C-0116` then located the threshold the verdict turns on: the crossing is at
  **`f` = 0.0788618807** and the measured interlayer coupling **0.26–0.33** clears it **monotonically** by
  **3.29690337×** at its adverse end. **The unspent axis was the BODY, not the coupling** — `C-0098` had
  genuinely closed the coupling. What survives is a 1.16× statement about a *coupled* four-layer tile under
  dropout, which is `T-197`.
- **The tall layer is dead, and it takes NDI's own objection with it.** §3's 100 pN stops arriving across a
  gap of **13.6989179 nm** at 0.5 mM — *below the bottom* of the 17–26 nm band NDI named — so a tall layer is
  refused at **96 of 96** states on §3's acceptable clause and admitted at **1 of 96** on the desired one
  (`C-0110`). It does not trade device A for device B: **it loses both.** It buys the *stroke* (52 of 96
  uncoupled) and not the *force* (`CH-0127`), and the concession is ours — counterion dominance is about ion
  **content**, never about the **decay**, and diluting makes NDI's estimate *optimistic*.
- **So the one reserve has ONE claimant, and the remaining question is a PRICE.** NDI's answers to decisions
  1 and 2 name the same reserve; the layer half is withdrawn by our own measurement, so what is left is the
  buffer, worth **1.75104168×** at the operating point on **three** routes that are **common mode** below
  `C-0005`'s 123–214 % one-loop error. `C-0114`, and `DECISIONS-FOR-NDI.md` now carries a consolidated §1+2.
  **This programme can rank what two spends BUY and has no column for what they COST.**
- **The electrode's unresolved quantity was a SIGN, not a magnitude.** `E_pzc(Au(111)) = 0.46–0.51 V vs SHE`,
  read directly (`C-0111`) — **90–576×** the deciding scale, and an electrode at zero volt on the *rational*
  scale is negatively charged and **lifts** the tile. What is open is the **cell's** definition of zero, filed
  as a one-line ask under decision 3.

**Suggested order.** `T-197` (is a *coupled* four-layer tile flat under the measured dropout? — the only
remaining piece of the flatness answer, and `C-0089`'s distribution and `C-0093`'s topology axes are worth
1.3–2.2× on exactly that statistic, neither searched on the thicker tile) then `T-198` (can four honeycomb
layers be rastered from one circular M13 at a buildable width? — the count fits, the raster rule does not
transfer, and every plan result in the corpus is single-layer square-lattice). `T-195` may reduce to a
statement now that `T-191` spends the scaffold remainder as thickness. `T-50` and `T-9` remain the two
genuinely expensive items and both are named and costed rather than dodged.

**What is still true from earlier notes.** The buildable tile is **38.08 nm**, not §3's 40.0 (`C-0086`,
`C-0095`), and it is 12 % flatter. The row-end crossover prestrain now has a derived value, **17.15–24.98°**
(`C-0107`), with **no** accessible measurement — the one published study of that coordinate excludes exactly
those sites in a scope clause — and `C-0112` shows the graded field over all 56 crossovers is **flat**
(0.0922622269) where the 14-site idealisation is not, the 42 interior sites carrying the *larger* half.

**Harness.** Run `tools/verify.sh` for anything authoritative — four post-Gradle gates, ~21 minutes. Four
retained checkers guard the documents and each has its own self-tests: `tools/trace-answers.py` (now four
checks, including challenge statuses), `tools/check-markdown-tables.py`, `tools/result-reader-census.py` and
`tools/reemission-order.py` (ask it before any re-emission sweep — a sweep is a topological **order**, not a
list). **Reserve claim, challenge AND task numbers in this file before starting parallel agents**; iteration
23 did and had **zero** collisions after six consecutive iterations of them. **Three concurrent agents, not
four**: four each running a `verify` and a `study` put this box at load 11.3 with 3 GB free and OOM'd the
Kotlin daemon despite the documented `-Xmx3g`, because that cure is per **daemon** and the constraint is the
**product**.

| P-15 | Repair `bracketedRoot`'s Illinois sign test | **DONE** (iteration 5) — claim `C-0031`, raises `CH-0043` | **There were two defects and the reported one is not the one that was costing anything.** The product sign test is real — and it also has a second half `C-0019` did not name, the *entry* test accepting a bracket with no root — but a product underflows only once **both** factors are below `1.5e-154`, and a direct probe of the unrepaired routine on residuals spanning 30 decades escapes **zero** times. What was active on **every** call is that **the Illinois halving was unconditional**: Dowell & Jarratt (read directly) halve only when the *older* endpoint is retained, and halving every step deflates both residuals so they interpolate to the midpoint — the method silently became **bisection while still paying for a secant**, 52 evaluations against bisection's ~52 on `x²−2` where correct Illinois takes **11**. The doc comment's "eightfold saving" was the whole justification for the method and it was delivering a *loss*. The two are coupled: the halving is the **amplifier** of the underflow. Diagnosis closes as a **theorem** — a secant through opposite-signed ordinates is a convex combination, so an escape *proves* a lost bracket. **Re-run and diff: no verdict, no window edge and no quoted figure moves anywhere.** Movement is one of exactly three things — one ulp (`T-1c`: 11 281 of 11 336 fields at a median `4.3e−16`), the SCF's own declared `HEIGHT_TOLERANCE = 1e-6` (`T-1f`: 589 fields, median `9.0e−7`), or a quantity that is **identically zero** (`T-1c`'s `equilibriumStiffness` at `L₀`, `\|value\| ≤ 2.0e−11 pN/nm`; `T-16`/`T-23`'s reproduction residuals at `~1e−9`). |

They are not changed because changing code that produces published results costs a re-run and a diff of everything downstream, and that price is only worth paying for a defect that can fire. | P-9 | **The effective `χ` of a *grafted* PEG layer is not the bulk one** — bound it, or declare `C-0002`'s bulk equation of state inapplicable to a brush | **DONE** (iteration 3) — **(a) inapplicable**; `C-0002`'s bulk equation of state **stands** | Claim `C-0013`, challenge `CH-0012`. **`χ ≈ 0.60` is not in the source.** Its fits are 0.789/0.852 on a scale whose *own* theta is **0.696** — the 0.60 was `1.2 × ½`, the ratio transferred onto the wrong axis, and the paper's disclaimer forbidding exactly that step is on the same page as the number. The system is an **air/D₂O Langmuir monolayer under *lateral* compression**, not a solid-grafted layer under normal compression. Its grafting densities **do** overlap the Gen-1 window, so the task closed on the *parameter*, not on system mismatch. Bounded independently at **`\|Δχ\| ≤ 0.053`** from normal-compression fits on grafted PEG at 1.5–2.5× the Gen-1 density: −11.4 % to +4.3 % in stiffness, inside `C-0003`'s own ±22 % bracket. |
| P-6 | `χ(T, salt)` and the Mg²⁺ salting-out coefficient for PEG/water at 2–10 mM | **DONE** (iteration 3) | Claim `C-0007`, challenge `CH-0006`. **The buffer does not reach the layer's mechanics**: the mobile-ion channel is exactly zero, and the solvent-quality channel is ≤ 0.4 % of the modulus over 2–10 mM. The Mg²⁺ coefficient is **not determinable and probably not well posed** — `θ(c)` shows *minima* for Group II chlorides, and PEG forms no binodal with MgCl₂ at all, so no ATPS-derived coefficient can exist. Bounded by a threshold instead: MgCl₂ would need `k_s ≥ 92.8 K/M`, 1.35× above the ceiling any PEO salt reaches. |

One grid discharges all three duties (lateral 60×, yaw 19×, hold-down 72×); **rows set by the load path, columns by flatness**. The zero is retired as a headline and kept as a free **20.2×**. |
| T-45 | The assembly tolerance of a 45-path DNA coupling | The relative stiffness scatter a staple-designed attachment array can be guaranteed to, against `C-0026`'s 0.883 pN per unit amplitude and its 17 % break-even | A1.2 | **ANSWERED as far as published measurement allows** (iteration 14) — [`C-0072`](gpd/claims/C-0072-plan-tolerance-model.md), [`CH-0084`](gpd/challenges/CH-0084-the-measured-staple-incorporation-is-past-the-flatness-threshold.md). **The measurement existed the whole time, under a different name.** `T-134`'s survey (77 recorded queries) found **Strauss et al., *Nat. Commun.* 9:1600 (2018)** — a DNA-PAINT map of incorporation efficiency at **single-staple resolution across all 168 staples** of a Rothemund rectangle, corroborated by next-generation sequencing: *"absolute incorporation efficiencies of **48–95 % with an average of 84 %**"*, **strongly position-dependent, worst at the edges**. A missing staple does not perturb a load path, it **removes** it, so the population is Bernoulli and `σ_rel = √(f/(1−f))`: the mean gives **43.6 %** relative per-path stiffness scatter — **1.26×** `C-0060`'s 34.6 % flatness threshold and **2.57×** `C-0026`'s 17 % break-even — the edge sites give **104 %**, and the centre sites **22.9 %**, which is inside both. **The mean alone, with no pattern assumption, is a 16 % shortfall on `C-0017`'s mandate**, 2.9× the worst rounding placement error `C-0060` spends a trim to remove. **And the position dependence runs the wrong way for `C-0058`**, which puts 34 of its 45 stations on the rim and gives them the stiff level. What is **still missing** is the stiffness spread of nominally identical *hybridised staple extensions* as such — nine queries across three databases found nothing, and the nearest published work (Videbæk et al., *PNAS* 2025) measures an inter-subunit ssDNA handle by cryo-EM multi-body refinement, which is the method that would supply it one level down. **What would close it**: a per-site incorporation measurement on a coupling-bearing tile (routine, by Strauss's own method), and `C-0060`'s flatness sweep re-run under a **position-dependent Bernoulli dropout** rather than an alternating amplitude. ~~TODO — **medium, and cheap**.~~ **`C-0060` (`T-122`, iteration 10) supplies the THRESHOLD the measurement is graded against**: `C-0058`'s flat design loses `T-5b`'s verdict at **34.6 %** relative scatter (31.6 % with `C-0017`'s total held), **2.04×** `C-0026`'s break-even and **0.52×** the 66.7 % at which the two stiffness populations merely *overlap* — so flatness binds at half the amplitude the ordering does, and a small scatter *improves* the dishing. It also finds that `C-0026`'s **build rule reverses** on a non-uniform coupling ([`CH-0073`](gpd/challenges/CH-0073-the-along-helix-scatter-rule-reverses-on-a-non-uniform-coupling.md)). What is still missing is the measurement itself. `C-0026` reports the sensitivity per unit amplitude precisely so a measured or specified tolerance can be substituted without re-running, but nothing accessible gives the stiffness spread of nominally identical hybridised staple extensions. It is also the only non-uniformity a *builder* controls, and `C-0026`'s build rule — **let the scatter alternate along the helices, not across them, where it restores exactly zero** — is worth stating in a design document whatever the number is. |

Read as a *regime* ceiling the derived number is **LOWER** than 0.2 and the device is **worse**: at 0.141 the coupled margin falls 0.563–2.464 → 0.168–1.660 and 20 coupled states drop below unity instead of 15. But **two-sidedness changes the currency**: the potential above `L₀` becomes quadratic, so the requirement is `k_BT/σ² = 0.4602 pN/nm`, which §3's own mandate exceeds **72.4× unpreloaded** — `F_req = k_req·σ` identically. On `C-0021`'s own balance the tetherless device goes from **1.4–5.4 `k_BT`, 0/18 confining** to **959–7582 `k_BT`, 18/18**, RMS 2.56–12.98 → 0.217–0.352 nm, so **`C-0014`'s eight substrate tethers leave the design**. The preload branch is priced and rejected: it is a **mounting offset**, i.e. a length, and the requirement asks for 0.0409 nm against a 0.34 nm base-pair quantum that delivers 9.3× too much. | T-30 | The origami joint at a flexure's end — does it draw in, and does it clamp? | The flexure's end condition and axial restraint fixed by a joint model rather than bracketed | A8.2 | **DONE** (iteration 4) — claim `C-0025`, raises `CH-0031`. **Both brackets survive as LIMITS of one joint, and `C-0023`'s restrained reading is the one realised.** `c(ρ) = 192(ρ+2)/(ρ+8)` is exactly 48 and 192 at its limits, so the "4×" bracket is a **continuum** — and every joint that **supports** the beam sits at `c = 83.2–191.7`, the **upper 76 %**. The axial bracket is decided by **anisotropy, not softness**: every covalent motif is isotropic, so a **direct crossover** gives span 44.03 nm, tangent **79.18 pN/nm — 1.98× past the 40 pN/nm ceiling — at every one of `k_s`'s four decades** and 61.0 pN of beam tension at §3's desired stroke. **`C-0023`'s 2 nt ssDNA hinge is falsified** (`CH-0031`): a flexible link has no direction, so it buys 1.30 nm of draw-in for **1.30 nm of transverse dead band**, 43 % of the stroke. **A double nick IS a crossover**, to the last digit. **The draw-in factor is 2.4 only at the endpoints** — interior minimum exactly **9/4 at ρ = 8**. **The design: 45 flexures, span 31.64 nm = 93 bp, on 8 nm = 24 bp NORMAL standoffs** (anisotropy `Sℓ²/3EI` = 102×), tangent **37.39 pN/nm**, tension 0.37 / 3.83 pN at 3 / 10 nm; window ℓ = 7–10 nm. `CH-0029` inverted: the restrained reading asks 86.7 pN, **past the 68.1 pN saturation — no bonded length carries it**. **`T-13` still closes.** |
| T-40 | Is a duplex standing normal to a single-layer sheet buildable, and what holds its base? | The base joint of `C-0025`'s normal standoff, modelled as one of its own motifs, against the clamp it assumes | A8.2 | **DONE** (iteration 4) — claim `C-0028`, raises `CH-0037`, `CH-0038`. **The base is not a clamp, the motif is not in the literature, and the window is re-cut rather than narrowed.** All three of `C-0025`'s standoff constants are the `ρ_b → ∞` limit of a **series**; at a crossover base `ρ_b = 0.18–0.59`, leaving **32.0 / 13.6 / 32.0 %** of them. A softer base **relieves** the compliance ceiling (tangent 33.8–38.5 pN/nm over the whole 3–10 nm range) and **destroys** stability: a single-crossover base **buckles at all eight lengths**. A pinned base with a free head is a **MECHANISM**, `P_c = 0` exactly — a corner the "factor of exactly 4" bracket does not contain — and **the standoff's sway IS the flexure's draw-in**, so the held-head reading is unavailable. What closes it is **orientation**: two crossovers laid *across* the flexure give **261.2 pN·nm/rad** against **27.06** laid along, **9.65× free**. **Design: span 31.06 nm = 91 bp on 8 nm standoffs with a two-crossover favourable base**, tangent **36.51 pN/nm**, buckling **1.41×** free-head, window **`ℓ = 7–9 nm`**. **`CH-0037`**: `C-0025`'s duty is the *mandate* secant, 1.27–1.70× low. **LITERATURE: the motif is NOT established** — every published out-of-plane base is a **pin**, and rigidity comes from **triangulation**. Fields et al.'s measured duplex buckling implies `L_p = 41.7 nm`, **25 % below CanDo**, so every `P_c` here is optimistic. **`k_s` now moves a verdict** — the first time in this programme. |
| T-65 | Does the standoff's off-diagonal compliance take the buckling margin below one? | The coupled 2 × 2 tip compliance of a standoff carrying the beam's end moment and its inward tension together, instead of two independent springs | A8.2 | **DONE** (iteration 4) — claim `C-0030`, raises `CH-0041` and `CH-0042`. **No: it takes it 1.55× ABOVE, and `C-0028`'s bound was right while its sign argument was wrong in both halves.** The dropped off-diagonal is not a compliance but a **draw-in the joint SUPPLIES** — the beam's end moment tilts the head inward, `Φδ` per end, **first order in the stroke against a second-order demand**, and **3.09×** larger than it at the design point. So the beam is in axial **COMPRESSION** over `0 < s < 9.9 nm` (`C-0023`'s cable term changes sign), the duty at 10 nm falls **5.113 → 3.313 pN**, and the free-head margin rises **1.41 → 2.18×** (CanDo) and **1.06 → 1.64×** (measured). Against a *net* demand the joint is **2.06× STIFFER**, not softer. **`c₀ ≡ c(ρ)` exactly**; the *effective* `c` becomes a function of the **stroke** (124.4 at 3 nm, 92.2 at 10). **But `Φδ` is odd where `e(δ)` is even, so the law is no longer ODD and the sign is a MOUNTING CHOICE**: favourable gives window **`ℓ = 5–10 nm`**, adverse gives 42–61 pN/nm and **no window at any length**. New: the flexure has its own buckling condition (`P7`, 3.1–4.2×), and the favourable mounting costs a **clearance** — §3's desired 10 nm stroke unreachable at any `ℓ ≤ 10 nm`. Design: **span 31.82 nm = 94 bp on 8 nm standoffs, tangent 25.23 pN/nm, margin 2.18×**. Maxwell-Betti asserted between two independently integrated off-diagonals, departure `0.0`. |
| T-75 | Which body carries the standoffs | The mounting sense of the flexure fixed by a buildable argument rather than left open | A8.2 | **DONE** (iteration 5) — claim `C-0035`, raises `CH-0045` and `CH-0046`. **SETTLED as a determination, and it was never a free binary.** The midspan is tied to one body and the ends stand on the other, so the deflection **is** the change in their separation: `dδ/ds = (v_base − v_driven)/n`, exactly `±1`, **with no length in it**. The sign is a **PRODUCT of two binaries** — base body **and** standoff normal — of which `C-0030` named one: of the four mountings exactly two are favourable, **one with each body and one with each normal**, so "which body" predicts the sign no better than a coin. Equivalently: favourable ⟺ the tie **crosses the base plane** ⟺ the flexure is **outboard** ⟺ the standoff is in **COMPRESSION**. **The survivor is unique — `Su`: standoff bases on the OUTPUT SUPERSTRUCTURE, standoffs pointing AWAY from the tile, flexure outboard above it, each midspan tied back DOWN through it to the tile.** The other favourable mounting (`Td`) puts the flexure inside the actuation gap: **37–85 % of the layer's own volume**, and at or below the electrode at §3's 5 and 7 nm layers, 0 of 9 admissible. Both adverse mountings fail `C-0023`'s ceiling **and** cannot place §3's effort point — inboard it cannot come closer to the tile than `ℓ`, and §3 allows **5 nm at the 10 nm layer on both readings of its band**. **The pre-bow escape is real, priced and rejected 12 of 12**: 4.08–16.66 nm of rise (12–49 bp) for a **150–225 pN preload = 1.5–2.25× §3's whole target force**, and **CLOSED outright at the desired stroke**. **The tile now carries no out-of-plane element at all — only `C-0015`'s 45 tie attachments.** |
| T-76 | Does a strain-SOFTENING coupling still satisfy `C-0017`'s stability condition? | `C-0018`'s pull-in and fold analysis re-run on a coupling whose tangent has an interior minimum BELOW its placed secant | A8.2 | **DONE** (iteration 5) — claim `C-0032`, **resolves `CH-0042` as UPHELD**, raises `CH-0047`. **NO at 2 mM, YES at 0.5 mM, and that is a design decision.** The placement clause is discharged **exactly** — the operating bias `V*` is identical across all four load lines at **144 of 144** comparisons, departure `0.0` — so the whole margin loss is in the ceiling. At 10 nm / 2 mM the pull-in bias falls only 0.7–1.8 %, but the **fold's own STROKE walks back from 3.41–4.13 nm to 2.80–3.17 nm**, through §3's 3 nm target at **two of six models**, and the bias margin collapses **1.007–1.032 → 1.0000–1.0019**: the device sits *on* its fold. `Q2` fails 6 of 6 (22.875 against a 23.41–27.91 floor). **`C-0017`'s theorem is confirmed where its premise holds** — the strain-*stiffening* decoupled reading loses **0 of 54** states and *raises* the margin to 1.020–1.774. **The escape fails: the adverse mounting is 42.38–61.04 pN/nm, 1.06–1.53× past `C-0023`'s 40 pN/nm ceiling at 0 of 8 standoff lengths.** At **0.5 mM** every predicate clears (1.44–5.93× on stiffness, 1.038–2.327× on bias), with one model acquiring a fold the mandate lacks. **A third route lands 2 % short**: `ℓ = 5 nm` raises the tangent minimum to 27.30 and clears five of six models; `ℓ = 4 nm` would clear all six and is excluded by `C-0030`'s `P4`. |

Nor can it be imperforate: the tie has to cross it at exactly the place the midspan descends toward, so **the aperture the tie needs and the clearance the midspan needs are one feature**. Priced from `C-0025`'s beam integrated once more, `w(u)/δ = (24u + 12ρu² − 16(2+ρ)u³)/(8+ρ)`: at §3's **acceptable 3 nm** stroke the cost is **exactly zero for `ℓ ≥ 6 nm`** — no penetration, no slot; at §3's **desired 10 nm** and `ℓ = 8 nm` the midspan goes **4.69 nm** past the contact plane and the beam demands a slot **18.37 nm long, 57.7 % of its own span, 54 bp**, which over 45 paths is **2223 nm² = 1.39× the tile's whole footprint**. **The escape from the ceiling is a hole bigger than the device.** An irreducible floor remains at every stroke: `C-0023`'s two-sidedness makes the tie a duplex, so 45 duplex-omission holes = **326 nm², 20.4 % of the footprint**. Whether the superstructure may be perforated is the one input this task cannot supply → **open question 6**, and `T-95`. |
| T-79 | A large-rotation two-spring elastica for `E5`'s arm | The arm placed on a composition that is exact in BOTH the rotation and the end condition | A8.2 | **DONE** (iteration 6) — claim `C-0039`, raises `CH-0053`. **The two compositions do NOT bracket the arm, and the desired stroke does not survive the geometry.** The elastica's vanishing-load limit reproduces `C-0034`'s `c(ρ_n, ρ_f)` at all four textbook corners and over a 25-point interior to **1.7e−14** — which pins the field equation, both boundary conditions and every sign at once — and on that solver `E5a16` places at **12.7198 nm = 37.4 bp, 1.79 % OUTSIDE the 11.028–12.496 nm bracket**. `C-0034`'s premise *"two errors run opposite ways"* is false: both are corrections to the same linear beam and both **stiffen** it. **The placement clause is discharged exactly (33.3333 pN/nm secant) and the compliance clause is not** — tangent **36.44 pN/nm at 3 nm** (inside the 40 pN/nm ceiling, 8.9 % to spare) and **264.2 pN/nm at 10 nm, 6.6× past it**, with the **secant** there already **69.94 = 2.10× the mandate** and the assembled force 699 pN against §3's 100. **The usable stroke inside the ceiling is 3.877 nm**, and `clears 10 nm` is **false at 0 of 34 placements and 0 of 25 sensitivity points** — including `C-0040`'s buildable hinge counts (1–6) and `C-0041`'s 15 paths, where the arm is 8.40 nm and the stroke is out of *geometric* reach. Cause: the arm is capped at 13.65 nm, so 10 nm is **≥ 73 % of its own contour**, and the exact draw-in is **5.34 nm = 15.7 bp** (42 %) against `C-0029`'s quoted 0.095. **An inextensible arm has no axially-held reading at all** — held, it must stretch, ≥ 299 pN, 4.6× past the 65 pN nicked ceiling. §3's **acceptable** stroke is untouched. |
| T-80 | Can `E5`'s arm end as a singly nicked continuation of a tile duplex? | A strand routing for the arm's far end, or the statement that only the two-terminus couple is available | A8.2 | TODO — medium. `C-0034` prices `A4` (one intact backbone, `k_θ` = 683 pN·nm/rad) as the motif whose **kinematics** match a guide — a nick preserves the helix axis, which is what *guided* means — and it takes the cap from 13.43 to **15.18 nm** and the realised `c` from 7.4 to **11.1**. | T-81 | Does a 16-crossover hinge line exist on a 40 nm tile? | The hinge count `C-0023`, `C-0029` and `C-0034` all take as given, checked against `C-0015`'s lattice | A8.2 | **DONE** (iteration 6) — claim `C-0040`, raises `CH-0054`. **NO, at every one of the 32 phases and in BOTH directions of the sheet.** A hinge line carries **four** crossovers, at every phase; the other parity carries **three** at the 22 seven-column phases and four at the 10 eight-column ones — and **the phases that maximise a hinge line are exactly `C-0015`'s ten centro-symmetric ones**, which nothing forced. Sixteen needs **163.2 nm = 4.08 tiles** of collinear interface, or **33 duplexes = 88.8 nm = 2.22 tiles** transversally — and the transverse line's crossovers are dihedral springs about the **wrong axis**, so `TASKS.md`'s own guess fails twice. Absolute geometric ceiling **six**; per flexure at 45 paths **one or two**; whole-tile inventory **49–56** against a demand of **720**, **12.86×**. **Sixteen crossovers CAN be assembled — four interfaces of four — but interfaces compose in SERIES: `n_eff = n_i·3(2m−1)/(m(2m+1))`, so they are worth 2.333 of hinge, 14.6 % of their count, and 45 such flexures give 16.03 pN/nm, 2.08× too soft.** Re-priced: the stroke needs **10** crossovers (12 inside the ceiling) against **3** for the ceiling at the acceptable stroke, so the window in hinge count is **`3 ≤ n ≤ 6`**. **At four the arm places at 7.748 nm — §3's acceptable 3 nm clears at 36.58 pN/nm, the desired 10 nm is out of geometric reach; at the one or two a flexure can own, the tangent is 42.0–54.1 pN/nm, past `C-0023`'s own ceiling.** The dominant compliance term changes back to the **hinge** (78 % at four, 95 % at one). Continuum control: the quantisation is worth −18 %/+9 % where the assertion is out **4.35×**, so it is a **density** fact, not a lattice one. Premise swept: 16 bp mis-reading → 8, honeycomb → 6, both → 12; **no reading reaches sixteen**. |
| T-99 | Does a coupling of FEWER, LONGER flexures close where 45 short ones do not? | The `(path count, hinge count, hinge-line length)` trade swept, or the statement that no point of it reaches §3's desired stroke | A8.2 | **DONE — [`C-0046`](gpd/claims/C-0046-fewer-longer-flexures.md), and the answer is NO at every point.** The trade is **degenerate on its own axis**: the arm the crossover inventory can place is `δ/sinθ` with `θtanθ = kδ²/(N k_θ)`, a function of the **product** `n·h` alone — **5.387 nm at all eight splits of `n·h = 56`** — so crossovers cannot be converted into arm length by moving them between hinges. What breaks the tie is `C-0034`'s far anchorage, which is **per-flexure**: the placed arm **grows** with the path count (6.903 nm at `(14,4)`, 8.231 at `(28,2)`, **9.973 at `(56,1)`**), so *fewer, longer* is the **wrong direction**. **0 of 31 points reach §3's desired stroke at the standing placement and 0 of 29 at its own, at BOTH readings of `C-0023`'s ceiling** — so `T-107` cannot move it. Best point `(56,1)`: arm 9.973 nm = 29.3 bp, tangent 38.17 pN/nm at 3 nm, **usable stroke 3.312 nm**, spending **100 %** of the tile's crossovers. Only **3** of 31 points clear even §3's acceptable stroke, all at `h = 1`. Raises [`CH-0059`](gpd/challenges/CH-0059-the-desired-stroke-placement-is-below-the-stability-floor.md). |
| T-110 | What does spending 80–100 % of the tile's crossovers on HINGES do to the sheet? | `C-0009`'s `D_⊥`, `C-0015`'s flatness and `C-0006`'s load distribution re-run on a sheet whose interfaces have been converted into free hinge lines | A8.2 | **DONE** (iteration 8) — claim [`C-0054`](gpd/claims/C-0054-consumed-crossover-sheet.md), raises [`CH-0066`](gpd/challenges/CH-0066-the-surviving-designs-consume-the-sheets-own-connectivity.md). **The two uses are EXCLUSIVE at the site** — `C-0040`'s own definition of a hinge line (one interface, one *pair of bodies*) and of `k_θ` as the *interhelical dihedral* spring settle it — **and the binding constraint is then not stiffness but TOPOLOGY.** A connected sheet needs one retained crossover on each of its 14 interfaces, so the hinge budget is **42 of 56 = 75.0 %** (35 of 49 at the other phases) against `C-0046`'s **80–100 %**: all three surviving designs sever the tile, into **≥ 4, ≥ 9 and exactly 15** pieces. **`D_⊥` does not degrade, it collapses** — the interfaces bend in **series**, so the honest rigidity is a harmonic mean and is **exactly zero** at the first empty interface where the smeared continuum still reports 0.667 pN·nm and an anisotropy of 128 instead of unbounded. Under `C-0022`'s solved load on the 3 × 15 grid: **0.218 intact → 0.242 at the ceiling (+11 %) → 0.465 at 45 (2.13×)** — **the step is at severance, not at consumption**, and the lattice/plate ratio flips 0.87 → 1.62 exactly there. The peak per-load-path force falls to **exactly zero** because a deleted path cannot be overloaded. **`C-0010`'s insensitivity is right and is the wrong channel**: the smeared plate moves 31 % where the lattice moves **117 %**, because a crossover is two elements and the *vertical link* is a constraint no plate can represent. The generous "a hinge IS an attachment" reading is **3.3× worse** at the same count. **The branch survives at `39 ≤ n ≤ 42`** — `C-0046`'s unresolved `34 < n ≤ 45` threshold is **39** — delivering 3.005–3.063 nm, and none of `C-0046`'s three designs is in it. **Converges with `C-0053` (`T-116`), filed the same iteration from the plan view: 42 is what the counting permits, 25 is what the geometry delivers, and the two bounds compose.** Opens `T-119` (the one falsifier: a junction site the single-layer sheet does not use) and `T-120`. |
| T-111 | A plan view for `E5a` — does the crossover-hinge flexure array pack? | `C-0041`'s blocking-digraph pipeline re-run on a body with no standoff and no tie | A8.2 | TODO — **medium-high.** `C-0041` solves the packing for the **standoff** flexure and gets 15; `E5a` has neither a standoff nor a tie, so that count does not transfer and no packing verdict for it exists. It matters because `C-0046`'s surviving design needs **45–56 paths** and would fail §3's *acceptable* stroke at 15 (2.469 nm). The pipeline is already written and the element is a plain `span × d` rectangle hinged on an interface, so this is cheap. **`C-0054` narrows the target: the count to pack is now 39–42, not 45–56.** **ANSWERED by [`C-0053`](gpd/claims/C-0053-hinge-arm-array-packing.md) (`T-116`, concurrent, iteration 8), which is the same question**: the bodies pack (0 clashes at every orientation, `arm + d = 11.82` under the 13.33 column pitch), the **hinge lattice** places **43** (three per interior row, two per edge row — exact, construction meets bound), and the host keeps every duplex bonded only up to **25** arms, because an arm is a length of duplex **cut out of** its own row and buries 11–13 further crossovers. `C-0053`'s 25 sits below `C-0054`'s 42 by exactly those two terms, so **the count to pack is 25, not 39–42**. What is left of `T-111` is the *stiffness* of that body, which is `T-120`. |
| T-119 | Can a flexure hinge be built on a junction site the single-layer sheet does not use? | A literature answer, or the statement that the motif does not exist — the one way `C-0054`'s exclusivity premise fails | A8.2 | **DONE** (iteration 9) — claim [`C-0055`](gpd/claims/C-0055-unused-junction-site.md), raises [`CH-0068`](gpd/challenges/CH-0068-the-hinge-inventory-is-not-the-sheets-own.md). **Answer (a): the site exists, it is published geometry, and `C-0054`'s pigeonhole falls — while `CH-0066`'s conclusion survives for a different reason.** Ke et al. (*JACS* **131**:15903, 2009, **read directly**): *"each double helix has up to four nearest neighbors … Every 8 bp, that staple strand is positioned to cross over to one of its four neighbors … adjacent helices share crossovers every 32 bp"*, and *"crossovers in i and iii … are parallel to the xz-plane, while … ii and iv … to the yz-plane"*. A single-layer sheet builds on **one of the two orthogonal plane families**; `8 bp × 33.75°/bp = 270.0°` **exactly**, so the empty family points **out of the sheet plane**. **The unused site is in BETTER register than the used one** — 4.286° against 8.571° at the preferred 10.5 bp/turn, exactly 2:1, because the departure is linear in the offset. **Census: 161–176 junction sites, of which the sheet builds 49–56 — it occupies 27.8–33.1 %, under a third at every phase**, and the in-plane half reproduces `C-0015`'s 56/49 as a **set equality** at all 32 phases from an independent construction. **So exclusivity AT A SITE is upheld and the INVENTORY premise is false**: the ceiling is not `56 − 14 = 42` with a severed tile but **52 upward hinges at `C-0054`'s own phase and 60 at the best, with all 56 interface crossovers retained and the sheet in ONE piece at every count**. **But the design count only moves `C-0053`'s 25 → 34**, because the escape and its price are the same fact — an upward line belongs to **one** duplex, so nothing empties *and* its roots sit at 10.88 nm against an arm demanding 11.82, three arms per row falling to two at `arm > 8.19 nm` (`n > 34`). **§3's 45 still does not place on a 40 nm tile — 30 do — for the root pitch, not for severance; at a 49.25 nm edge 45 places with the host intact.** New phase trade: the ten phases maximising the upward inventory (0–2, 14–18, 30, 31) are **disjoint** from `C-0015`'s ten that maximise the interface one. **The motif is half-published**: the site and a crossover on it are the elementary step of square-lattice multilayer origami, but a **free lever** held by one crossover there was **NOT FOUND** in 62 recorded EuropePMC queries across fifteen families, every published origami hinge is an **ssDNA** connection (2023 review, read directly), and Ke et al. name the **8 bp staple break** it forces as a *"significantly lower yield"* cost. Opens `T-121`. |
| T-120 | Is a sheet held together by ONE crossover per interface still a plate? | `C-0009`'s discreteness criteria re-derived at the connectivity ceiling | A8.2 | **DONE** (iteration 9) — claim [`C-0056`](gpd/claims/C-0056-connectivity-ceiling-plate.md), raises [`CH-0069`](gpd/challenges/CH-0069-the-plate-fails-before-the-connectivity-does.md). **NO, and it stops being one FIVE crossovers before it stops being connected.** The depleted lattice is parametrised by **one** number through `p_eff = p N/N_ret` and `D_⊥ = k_θ d/p_eff`, both exact at `N_ret = N`, so every criterion is `C-0009`'s published number times an exact power of `N_ret` and **each therefore INVERTS**. At the ceiling an anchor's influence patch holds **0.694** crossovers against 3.93 intact, and **inverted rather than evaluated the patch criterion crosses one at 37.3 SPENT — inside `C-0054`'s own `39 ≤ n ≤ 42` window.** The other two criteria are useless here: `ℓ_⊥/d` reads **1.061**, above one, and never fires; `ℓ_∥/p_eff` demands **67.2** crossovers, more than the sheet owns, so it is **degenerate** — it fails on the intact sheet at every foundation but the softest. **`ℓ_⊥/d` cannot tell depletion from a stiffer foundation at all** (both divide `D_⊥/k_f`; the ceiling's 1.061 IS `C-0009`'s `k_f×4` corner, exactly), where the other two separate the same pair by `2√2`. **A criterion is not a model**, so a third model — fifteen uncoupled beams on one foundation — was run beside the plate: **the four thresholds fall in a strict order, 28 (point compliance) < 37.3 (criterion) < 42 (connectivity) < 45 (smooth load), 1.61× apart.** `C-0009`'s smooth/point split **survives and becomes a change of model class**: the plate is out 14.9 % on `C-0022`'s solved load at the ceiling and **33–79 % under a concentrated lever**, and the point compliance varies **2.53×** over one cell where the plate says 1.14× — a quantity the plate does not have. `C-0006`'s load distribution stands (the hinges carry `9e−4` of the energy **intact**); **`C-0010`'s insensitivity survives and strengthens** (3.07 % → **0.99 %** at the ceiling) while the amplitude rises 1.71×, so it is insensitivity to the *rigidity* and not to the *connectivity*; `C-0047`'s 0.218/0.695 and `CH-0034`'s saturation are unchanged. **Found and bracketed: `C-0054`'s `SPREAD` tie-break clusters all 14 survivors into the two lowest columns** — a `staggeredRetention` is 1.68× better in the distance from an anchor to the nearest surviving crossover at identical count and density. |
| T-121 | What do 34 duplexes stacked ABOVE the tile do to it? | `C-0009`'s grillage, `C-0006`'s flatness and `C-0010`'s variance re-run on a sheet carrying `C-0055`'s upward arm array | A8.2 | **DONE** (iteration 10) — claim [`C-0061`](gpd/claims/C-0061-stacked-arm-sheet.md), raises [`CH-0074`](gpd/challenges/CH-0074-the-flat-distribution-lives-on-stations-no-placement-supplies.md). **They do EXACTLY NOTHING to the sheet's rigidity, and the reason is the same sentence that limited them to 34.** A body attached to a structure at **one** point and otherwise free has a **zero Schur complement** there — its rigid-body motions span everything one crossover can impose — so 34 arms covering **46.3 %** of the tile's plan move `C-0009`'s peak dishing, peak crossover force and peak duplex shear by `5e−9` relative, and that residual is the arm regularisation and vanishes **linearly** with it over four decades. **The single tie is forced, not chosen**: an upward site belongs to one duplex, so its pitch is the bare 32 bp = **10.88 nm**, against an arm of **8.164 nm** at 34 paths and **9.131 nm even at §3's 45**. The bracket has **three** steps and none is large — one tie adds nothing; **two ties add a TORSION BAR and nothing else** (5.832 pN·nm/rad, **0.112 %** of the dishing, because two points determine a line); the arm's own `EI` needs **three** ties, i.e. 21.76 nm of arm; the 19.3× axially coupled second layer is outside both the motif and the kinematics. **Mass: 1.21× on a quality factor of `5e−4`.** **Drag is the only channel that moves anything and it is an upper bound** — the arms are on the `+z` face, so `C-0004`'s squeeze film is untouched **by construction**, and what they add is **9.1 %** of the total drag: the nominal corner goes 91.2 → **82.9 kHz** and the **worst** §4(d) margin 22.81× → **20.73×**, still discharged. **`C-0010`'s `σ_RMS ≤ 3.0 nm` is IDENTICALLY unchanged broadband** — equipartition sees no drag and no mass — with the in-band amplitude up **4.9 %**. **What the array really changes is where the coupling ENTERS**: on `C-0055`'s own 34 roots a uniform coupling dishes **0.4156** of the stroke against 0.2182 on `C-0015`'s 3 × 15 and **0.3079** free — **1.35× worse than no coupling at all** — and `C-0058`'s flat rim rule reaches only **0.1649** there against its published 0.0753 (`CH-0074`). Two smaller results: **`C-0055`'s placement is not centro-symmetric** (centroid at `x = −8.80 nm`) and reflecting alternate rows is free and worth 0.4156 → 0.3558; and the peak crossover force is **8.3×** larger at the arm roots than on the inset grid. Opens `T-125` and `T-126`. ~~TODO — **high, and it is the largest open item `C-0055` leaves.**~~ `C-0055` moves the `E5a` array off the sheet's own crossovers and onto the unoccupied upward azimuth, which costs the host **no crossover and no duplex length** — so every table in `C-0054` (computed on *consumed* interfaces) simply does not apply, and nothing replaces them. But 34 arms of 8.16 nm are **278 nm of duplex added out of plane at one interhelical distance**, i.e. mass, drag and rigidity in a direction no model in this programme contains: `C-0009`'s grillage is a single sheet, `C-0006`'s plate is 2-D, and `C-0010`'s variance budget has no out-of-plane member. The cheap bound first — the arms' plan footprint is 0.46 of the tile and their added second moment about the sheet's mid-plane is `A d²`, which is knowable before any solve. Also unresolved: the **8 bp staple break** Ke et al. name as a yield cost has a published sign and no published size at 34 arms per tile, and the caDNAno scaffold route that visits them (35.7 % of M13 is a ledger, not a route). |
| T-122 | Can a 5:1 per-path coupling stiffness ratio be BUILT? | `C-0030`/`C-0023`'s element catalogue asked for two stiffnesses on one tile, or the statement that a coupling can only be uniform | A8.2 | **DONE** (iteration 10) — claim [`C-0060`](gpd/claims/C-0060-buildable-stiffness-ratio.md), raises [`CH-0073`](gpd/challenges/CH-0073-the-along-helix-scatter-rule-reverses-on-a-non-uniform-coupling.md) against `C-0026`'s build rule. **YES on the stiffness, NO on the placement — and the qualifier `C-0058` needs is about the ARRAY, not the ratio.** The declared falsifier — *one quantum of the design parameter taking the ratio out of the flat window* — **did not fire at any of fourteen element/level pairs**: the coarsest quantum is **19.1 %** of a level's own stiffness (an 11 bp hinge arm), the finest **1.0 %** (a 99 nt ssDNA limb), against a flat ratio window this task **measures rather than cites** at **3.5 ≤ R ≤ 20**, i.e. 471 % wide — quantisation is **25× finer than the requirement**, where `C-0023`'s mounting-offset quantum was **8.3× coarser** than its own. **The structural reason is one line**: a preload is a *length* and a stiffness is a *power* of a length, so the same 0.34 nm enters divided by 87 base pairs rather than by one. All seven settings reach both levels (realised ratios **4.667–5.144**) and **all fourteen built designs are flat**, 0.0715–0.0815 against `T-5b`'s 0.10. **A second cheap bound turned out to be load-bearing**: rounding the two *levels* independently misses `C-0017`'s mandate by **0.40–5.44 %** — a placement error, not a rounding nuisance — and **trimming individual paths by ONE base pair** (legitimate because the mandate is an equality on a **sum**) takes the worst to **`1.3e−4`** in ≤ 18 moves, at the price of 3–4 distinct staple lengths instead of two. **`T-45` is answered as a THRESHOLD**: the built design loses flatness at **34.6 %** relative scatter (31.6 % with the total held), **2.04×** `C-0026`'s 17 % break-even and **0.52×** the 66.7 % at which the two populations merely *overlap* — so **flatness binds at half the amplitude the ordering does**, and small scatter *helps*. **What fails is the array**: `k ∝ span^(−3)` makes the soft level **1.71×** the longer member, so `C-0030`'s interior span is **52.36 nm** on a 40 nm tile and places **0** of 45; `E3f` 15, `E5n1` 30, `E5n2` 15, `C39` 0 — six of seven — and only `E4` places 45, at the second ground `C-0023` already excluded it for. **A small free result: the best one-parameter ratio at `C-0058`'s own collar is 7, not 5** (0.0653 against 0.0753, a further 13.4 %), and it is no harder to build. Opens `T-127` (does a MIXED-span array pack?). ~~TODO — **high, and it is the largest open item `C-0058` leaves.**~~ `C-0058` makes the Gen-1 tile flat for the first time by giving the 34 rim stations 5× the stiffness of the 11 interior ones at `C-0017`'s unchanged total — but every distribution in it assumes an independently specifiable linear spring per station, which no claim has ever had to supply. Also to check: `CH-0042`'s strain-softening is a **different curve per span**, so two spans do not stay in a 5:1 ratio over the stroke, and `C-0058`'s springs are linear. **If the answer is no, `CH-0071` stands only with the qualifier "for buildable couplings" and `CH-0034`'s floor returns.** |

| T-132 | Does the leg's own length budget survive the PINNED base misalignment? | `C-0052`'s `chordPairMisalignment(m)` re-composed against `C-0065`'s registered base misalignment at one global leg length, over the whole 12–26 step envelope — or the statement that no leg length serves all 34 instances | A8.2 | **DONE** (iteration 13) — claim [`C-0070`](gpd/claims/C-0070-pinned-leg-budget.md), raises [`CH-0082`](gpd/challenges/CH-0082-a-truss-has-two-legs-and-the-design-table-gives-it-one-base.md), discharges [`CH-0078`](gpd/challenges/CH-0078-the-base-floor-is-a-minimum-over-a-coordinate-the-array-pins.md)'s item 2 and **upholds it**. **YES — the budget survives, at one leg length for all 34 instances, and the shared-length clause costs NOTHING.** Cheap bound 1 decides the shape: `C-0065`'s phase-class congruence carries from the station to the **68 leg bases**, which fall into exactly **2 classes of 34** presenting **1** distinct `(low leg, high leg)` pair at every row pitch — so *"one length for 34 instances"* collapses to *"one length for two legs"*, and the declared falsifier did not fire. What pinning actually does is make the base **OVERSPEND** `C-0052`'s budget rather than spend it exactly: a free rotation always opposes the budget's sense and a pinned one need not, so `ψ_base + ψ_cap ≥ \|m τ − 90°\|` **always**, and at the recommended design the two ends spend **81.13°** of a **45.13°** budget (**1.80×**). **And nothing fails** — at the 9 bp row **all 15** leg lengths of the 12–26 envelope are representable and pass all nine predicates, at margins **1.815–2.443** (CanDo) and **1.364–1.836** (Fields). The best shared length is **12 steps, 4.08 nm**, at **every** surviving row; at 9 bp it carries **2.443 / 1.836**, *better* than `C-0062`'s own 2.410 / 1.812 there (a worse cap moves couple into the plane that governs) and **0.13 % below** the 2.446 of the 10 bp row the array cannot build. **`C-0065`'s 17 of 44 and its 9 bp / 18.0° recommendation are unchanged** — the tightening is zero. One new constraint appears and does not bind: a truss has **two** legs pinned at **two** azimuths (9.0° and 18.0° at 9 bp), so their cap chords differ by that angle at every length, giving a floor `\|fold(δ_A−δ_B)\|/2` — 4.5° here, but **28.5°** at the 6 and 10 bp rows against `C-0062`'s composed 9.0° and 27.0° (`CH-0082`). One sensitivity moves the verdict: a **0.5 nm lateral seat** leaves the 9 bp row not representable at any leg length. ~~TODO~~ |

The two-support flexure family's **shortest possible** member is `(48 EI/k)^(1/3)` = **22.41 nm — 2.74×** the budget, so it is refused **at every span, every end joint and every placement**, which is strictly stronger than `C-0065`'s 12 of 34. And the normal direction supplies only **axial** stiffness: `S/k` = **1122 nm**, 112× `C-0017`'s whole envelope. **What refuses the flexure is its END CONDITION**: `c` is 48–192 supported twice and ≤ 12 supported once, and the span is `c^(1/3)`. The other escapes fail too — a **fold** shrinks the budget (28 of 34 at the contour where the straight element places 34), a second **level** is reached only by a vertical member whose clash is level-independent (`C-0041`), and a **single strand** is compact (6.04 nm) and places and is **one-sided**, measured at negative argument. **The budget reduces to one inequality, `c ≤ 2.3416`, and both of the arm's joints sit inside it by 2–3 %**: at a one-crossover root the tip may be no stiffer than **79.68** and `C-0034`'s `A2` is **78.24**; at an `A2` tip the root may be no stiffer than **13.93** and one crossover is **13.53**. Neither joint was chosen for that reason and **the design has no margin on either**. The window has a **floor** as well, `1.5×` the stroke = 4.50 nm — a pinned tip at a one-crossover root asks for 3.591 nm and is **refused by its own kinematics, short enough to place and too short to work**. **Three sensitivities close the 0.0256 nm margin**: the 2.73 nm square-lattice interhelical distance (18 of 34), the top of Chen et al.'s own fitted `α` bracket (30 of 34), and §3's 45 paths (24 of 34) — so **34 is the count the plan budget prefers**, not a rounding of 45. `C-0065`'s 27.4119472 nm span and its **12 of 34** both reproduce at departure `0.0`. ~~TODO~~ |

**`0.0`**, and **independently of the amplitude** (swept 0 → 2.5 nm at `≤1e−14`) — which **annihilates the whole disputed 1.2–1.75 nm amplitude bracket**. `C-0065`'s one-phase-class congruence extends minimum IS the steric floor**. **What the measurement reopens is the width's VALUE**: the placement threshold is the lattice quantity `pitch − arm = 2.715609 nm` and the defensible readings **straddle it** — 34 of 34 at the measured contact, at 2.0 and at 2.69; **22** at Bai's midpoint 2.725, the square 2.73 and oxDNA's 3.25. (2019, already in `gpd/data/T-151-sources/`) measure the interduplex corrugation of a 2D origami by oxDNA at ~8-9 deg average and ~+-16 deg per junction and write *"we ... exclude the outermost junctions on the tile"* — and their tile is **twist-corrected** (31/32 bp mix) where `C-0086`'s 112 bp raster is not. **So the oxDNA run is WARRANTED and it fits this box**: one row end plus two neighbours, **~256 nt** against `T-9`'s own *"2-5 k nucleotides ... days not weeks"*, protocol published, no GPU. 8 ceilings, 12 boundary-layer cells, 11 solved states, 8 literature records, 5 convergence records, 7 reproductions, 6 falsifiers; **16 gate-named tests**; `C-0090`'s **0.0621469105** reproduced at **3.0e-08** on an INDEPENDENT explicit-elastic-support path (no influence bank, so `CH-0120`'s trap cannot fire); result file re-run through `tools/study.sh` and **byte-identical**; `tools/verify.sh` **BUILD SUCCESSFUL in 21 m 34 s**, no test failure, with only a sibling's mid-TDD `coupling/CountPhaseInteractionStudy.kt` dropped. **`F6` fired once and was right to**: the standing uniform-load falsifier read 0.170 on the 34-anchor coupled lattice — a sparse coupling IS a dishing source (`C-0060`) — and reads **2.126e-07** where it belongs, on the support-free lattice. | T-190 | **What do the 42 INTERIOR crossovers carry, and does their cancellation hold?** | The graded corrugated field `(-1)^b u(x)` re-read over the interior sites separately from the 14 row-end ones, with the cancellation decomposed | A8.2 | **DONE** (iteration 23) — [`C-0112`](gpd/claims/C-0112-interior-crossover-prestrain.md). **The field is exactly separable and the verdict is not.** A prestrain is a load, so the split into 14 + 42 is an IDENTITY (**2.1e-15** in the coefficient vector); peak dishing is a **seminorm** and does not add (the graded peak is **0.294** of the sum of its parts'), so the cancellation is a **cross term**, `cos = -0.579495374`. **The 42 interior sites are the larger half** — **53.65 %** of the assembled absolute couple, `\|I\|/\|R\| = 0.688` — and the cancellation is structural and needs no solve: the column next inboard carries **0.661** of the row end's amplitude at the **opposite** sign, and one column pair recovers **106.2 %** of the whole move. **`C-0107`'s 0.0922622 SURVIVES**, reproduced at **1.1e-10**, flat at **both** overall signs (0.0922622 / **0.0910197**) and at **40 of 40** bracket cells against **14 of 40** for the row-end-only idealisation. | T-152 | **What stacking-prevention clearance should the collinear slot carry, and does the whole branch's plan budget move?** | A collinear clearance quantised at the 0.34 nm rise, with the `Q5` margin, the `c ≤ 2.3416` joint window and `C-0053`'s 43-of-45 all re-read at it — or the statement that the allowance is a fabrication number this programme cannot set | A8.2 | **DONE** (iteration 17) — claim [`C-0085`](gpd/claims/C-0085-collinear-stacking-clearance.md), task [`T-152`](gpd/tasks/T-152.md), raises [`CH-0100`](gpd/challenges/CH-0100-the-collinear-margin-is-an-integer-not-a-residue.md). **SIX BASE PAIRS, 2.04 nm — and the cheap bound reshaped the question before any code ran: the MARGIN IS AN INTEGER, not a residue.** A collinear gap is an **axial** length between two duplex end faces, so it is quantised at the rise like the other two terms, and `M = (32 − N_d − N_L)` rises. The published **0.02561 nm** is what is left when a **transverse** SAXS constant (**7.912** rises) and an elastica root (**24.013** rises) are subtracted from an integer pitch; on the lattice the standing design closes at **exactly zero**, and quantising the convention *up* to the 8 rises a design can draw makes the margin **negative** and drops the array to **30 of 34**. **The criterion is an energy and the geometry supplies it**: both faces are tethered to the same sheet, so a stack costs the elastic work of closing the gap, and holding the stacked state below one per cent on the softest closure path (30.518 pN/nm) asks **1.90518 nm**. The adopted ladder spans **4/5/5/6** rises across a 2× closure stiffness and a 100× occupancy, so the whole modelling exposure is two rises and **the design as built already carries 8**. Margin **0.67561 nm = 2 WHOLE RISES, 26.38×** the published one. **All three of `C-0071`'s live `NONE` bands become real margins** — the plan length at 2 whole rises, and: tip 79.678 → **133.687** (**1.7088×** `A2`, from 1.0184×), root 13.930 → **25.689** (**1.8988×** one crossover, from 1.0296×), `c ≤ 2.34166` → **2.94462**. Nothing moves adversely — 34 of 34 at one level, `C-0053`'s 45-arm count **43 → 43**, `C-0074`'s 30-root ceiling **9.535 → 9.86 nm** — and `C-0069`'s midspan-family negative survives a clearance of **zero** (22.414 nm against a bare 10.88 pitch). **Two surprises.** The **conservatism is load-bearing**: a rigid-rooted arm (9.247 nm) *places* at 4 rises or fewer, so the loose end of the stacking range would reopen the truss branch `CH-0081` closed. And **distance is the only control the design still owns** — all three of Rothemund's measured anti-stacking remedies need a strand **terminus**, and `C-0034`'s `A2` has spent both of the two a duplex end has (`C-0029`'s counting theorem, striking twice on the same links). No falsifier fired. |
| T-140 | Does the Rothemund SEAM break `C-0076`'s node congruence? | A weave phase model carrying the scaffold seam, checked against `C-0063`'s 34 stations — or the statement that no station lies within one weave period of a seam | A8.2 | **DONE** (iteration 16) — claim [`C-0081`](gpd/claims/C-0081-seam-weave-congruence.md). **YES, wherever it sits, and the acceptance's second branch is answered NO.** A seam does not perturb the weave, it **deletes an extremum**: at any even plane every duplex participates in exactly one interface crossover, so removing the junctions at plane `s` removes exactly **one pull event from every duplex**, every duplex is straight at `±Δ/4` from `s−2` to `s+2`, and every interface is pinned at `mean ± Δ/2` instead of passing through its mean at the odd planes. The 40 nm tile is **3.68 weave periods** wide and admits **8** seam planes; every one of them puts **6–12 of the 34 stations** inside its straight window (**12–20** within a full period), so **22–28 of 34** survive on a node against 34 with no seam. There is nowhere for a seam to hide. **`C-0076`'s VERDICT still stands, on its other argument** — `M = p − d − L` is an along-helix identity between unbonded bodies and carries no weave coordinate, so the plan margin is **0.898333453 nm at all 8 positions, one distinct value**. `CLAUDE.md`'s *a verdict that survives can survive on a different reason*, and the first instance where the surviving reason was named in advance by the claim being qualified. **What the seam costs is `C-0076`'s headline**: the departure at an affected station is exactly `Δ/2`, unit slope, so the annihilated **1.2–1.75 nm amplitude bracket is restored at full strength** — 0.600 / 0.750 / 0.875 nm at Yoo / Snodin / Bai. **And the sign is the opposite of the wording**: *opens up* is the favourable half; the other parity **closes** by the same amount, so the worst across-row clearance at a station falls 0.872724 → **0.122724** nm at Snodin's amplitude and to **−0.002276 nm** at Bai's — a clash, at the measured phosphate girth, which `T-139` can only make worse. **The model reproduces Snodin's own sentence without being told to**: one group of pairs at the maximum and *the other … opens up much less* at the minimum, from the shared duplexes' geometry alone, with no appeal to the *extra scaffold crossovers* he attributes it to — the only independent check the model has. **The remedy is free and is an INPUT**: this programme's tile has no scaffold routing, and a seamless one restores `C-0076` in full. Raises no challenge — `C-0076` named this counter-case itself. Opens `T-151`. |
| T-151 | **Can the Gen-1 tile be raster-folded WITHOUT a scaffold seam?** | A scaffold routing for a 15-duplex 40 nm single-layer sheet with no seam, or the statement that one is unavailable at this aspect ratio | A8.2 | **DONE** (iteration 17) — claim [`C-0086`](gpd/claims/C-0086-seamless-scaffold-routing.md), task [`T-151`](gpd/tasks/T-151.md), raises [`CH-0101`](gpd/challenges/CH-0101-the-nominal-tile-width-is-not-a-buildable-raster-width.md). **YES — and a seam is a PARITY ON A TREE, not a fabrication convention.** Crossovers join only *adjacent* duplexes, so the row-adjacency graph is the path `P_D`, a **tree**, and a closed walk on a tree traverses every edge an **even** number of times: a **fully folded circular** scaffold therefore gives every row two segments, which is exactly Rothemund's seam. Brute-forced, `P_D` carries **2 Hamiltonian paths and ZERO Hamiltonian cycles** at every `D` from 3 to 12. **So a seam needs BOTH premises — circular AND fully folded — and the Gen-1 tile fails the second anyway**, taking **1 680** of M13's **7 249** nt. The seamless routing is the plain boustrophedon, and **Rothemund built it twice**: a 26-helix square that *"had no vertical reversals in raster direction, required a linear scaffold"*, and — his **first** origami experiment — an 8-helix raster on a **circular** M13 with two thirds unfolded, *"long, unfolded single-stranded sections of the scaffold do not adversely affect folding"*. Both read directly. **Three things the programme had not counted.** The **remainder** is a body: 5 569 unpaired nt is a **33.3 nm** coil carrying **1.66×** the sheet's own backbone charge in `C-0022`'s gap, and a purpose-built 1 680 nt scaffold removes it (a circular one needs a **67 nt** return loop). **Rothemund's own staggered seam is NOT the remedy** — solved exactly over the three-row coupling `C-0081`'s mechanism implies, the best stagger still costs **5 of 34** against a straight seam's 6–12, worth one station. And a seamless raster **quantises the tile's WIDTH at 32 bp**: the admissible row lengths are odd multiples of the 16 bp crossover spacing — 16, 48, 80, 112, 144 bp — and **§3's 40.0 nm (117.6 bp) is not among them**; the nearest is **112 bp = 38.08 nm**, 4.8 % narrower (`CH-0101`). F5 fired. `C-0081`'s 8 planes and 6–12 cost reproduce from its station planes alone. |

| T-161 | **Can a crossover be drawn at the LAST base pair of a duplex?** | A literature answer, or a design-language one, on whether a scaffold or staple crossover may sit at a row end — with the placement verdict it decides stated beside it | A8.2 | **DONE** (iteration 19) — claim [`C-0095`](gpd/claims/C-0095-row-end-crossover.md), task [`T-161`](gpd/tasks/T-161.md), result `gpd/results/T-161-row-end-crossover.json`, sources and query log `gpd/data/T-161-sources/`, raises [`CH-0111`](gpd/challenges/CH-0111-the-row-end-crossover-is-admitted-with-an-interior-crossover-s-stiffness.md), **closes `C-0090`'s open item 2**. **YES, AND IT IS NOT A PERMISSION — IT IS THE DEFINITION OF A RASTER TURN.** Rothemund's odd-half-turn rule binds *"the distance between successive scaffold crossovers"*, and in a boustrophedon those are the **two ends of one row**: the 112 bp `C-0086` quantised **is** the crossover-to-crossover distance, so a crossover at the last base pair is what makes the row 112 bp long. **The cheap bound was the whole answer and it cost a `grep`** — the primary source was already in the tree, fetched by `T-151`. **Three headings, decided separately.** *The geometry does not forbid it*: a duplex end carries **2** strand termini and the 33.74°/bp quantum lets one base pair reach **one** neighbour, so it offers **1** crossover — and a boustrophedon demands **exactly 1** at every row end it uses, leaving **two** free (the scaffold's own termini, one per edge on an odd row count). A crossover is an **azimuthal** condition and *"the last base pair"* an **axial** coordinate; they are independent, and the terminus is *less* constrained than an interior site. *The software does not forbid it*: caDNAno *"permits the user to force crossovers between any two staple bases or between any two scaffold bases"*, cadnano 2.1 **automates** the raster turn, and scadnano's API states *"an xover is necessarily at an enpoint of a strand"*. *And it is published*: **F5 FIRED, FAVOURABLY** — Rothemund's 24-helix rectangle is **288 bp = 18 column pitches EXACTLY** (Suppl. Fig. S19, *"27 turns wide at 10.666 bases / turn -> 288 nt"*), so **both** vertical edges lie on the crossover lattice, and it folded **90 % well-formed**. His Suppl. Note S2 contemplates the case verbatim — *"even where a seam or edge lines up with the underlying crossover lattice"* — and reports *"the last base pair does form and assumes a planar configuration"*. **The unexpected result is a theorem**: `C-0086`'s odd-half-turn rule and the row-end column **parity** condition are the SAME congruence, asserted identical over **1–400 bp** — a boustrophedon needs complementary parities at its two edges, and that is exactly an **odd** pitch count. So the eight columns at phase 8/24 carry `C-0015`'s **56** as **14 scaffold raster turns + 42 staple crossovers**, the 14 being `C-0086`'s own LINEAR count recovered from the lattice; and the model reproduces a structure it was not told about (an **even** pitch count puts one parity on both edges, which is what a seamed **double** raster demands). **VERDICT: the programme carries `C-0090`'s ADMITTED reading, 0.0621469105** — against **0.168371808** at the same phase (**2.70925468×**) and 0.156510532 at the best refused phase (2.51839602×) — so the 38.08 nm tile is **inside** `T-5b`'s 0.10 and **12.0 % flatter** than §3's nominal 40.0 nm at 0.0706145537. `CrossoverLayout.EDGE_MARGIN` is a numerical guard and must not be read as a physical assertion. **NO SIXTH NDI QUESTION** — the literature is not silent, and what is left is mechanics: Rothemund says the edge crossover's strain is unrelieved and *"how the strain is actually relieved is unknown"*, so the two *conventions* are in fact a stiffness **BRACKET** on 14 of the 56 crossovers, and `T-5b`'s 0.10 sits **0.356** of the way across it (`CH-0111`, `T-164`). F1–F4 and F6 silent; the upward station lattice is identical under both conventions (52 / 53). |

Two interpolated intermediate gaps move the design reading by **exactly zero**, and the tightest range's 41/81/161 sampling spread is 0.08 %. **The cheap bound did not fire** (per-state floor 0.0056, 18× below) and `C-0064`'s sign instrument explains it: a 10 nm device's own two ends run **+0.9969 to +0.9998**, while the 5 nm device's two states run **−0.9427** — a device whose range is anti-parallel to itself. `C-0064`'s subset dichotomy transfers in **direction, not exactness**: 12 of the 14 mixed subsets fail (against 14 of 14 on the grid). ~~TODO — **high, and it is the cheapest open item on the branch.**~~ `C-0063` makes the Gen-1 tile flat at **0.0706** of the stroke with **equal springs** on a placement the upward lattice actually supplies, which resolves `CH-0074` — but it reports that at **one** state, `C-0022`'s 2 mM / 10 nm / 0.192 V design point, which is the same state `C-0058` was tuned at. `C-0064` (`T-123`) is the reason that is not enough: a flatness verdict at one state does not travel, the uniform coupling on the 3 × 15 grid moves from 0.2182 to 0.0710 across `C-0022`'s five, and the 2 nm state's edge effect has the **opposite sign** to every 10 nm state's. Everything needed exists: `coupling.MultiStateSurrogate` prices every state of one distribution on `n + S` load cases and `minimaxStiffnessDistribution` optimises the worst of them, both model-agnostic; `anchoring.UpwardRootPlacement` supplies the stations. Three questions in one run — is `C-0063`'s **winning placement** flat over the 2 mM device's traversed range; if not, does a **different phase** of the same lattice do better (the phase sweep is over a *placement*, and `C-0063` shows all ten eight-column phases reach 0.077–0.092 at one state); and does the **34-root** array need a distribution after all, given `C-0058`'s rim rule reverses sign there and `C-0063`'s own 34-parameter optimum wants a peak ratio of only 1.30. The falsifier is that the best placement misses `T-5b`'s 0.10 over the range, in which case the flat Gen-1 tile is a single-state result and `CH-0074` reopens on a new axis. |

The threshold: an imperforate superstructure caps the stroke at `ℓ − 2.69 nm`, i.e. **5.31 nm at the recommended `ℓ = 8 nm`**, which covers §3's acceptable clause and nothing more. **Carried to the open questions for Kazik as item 6.** |
| T-96 | Does the surviving mounting survive `T-31`'s array packing? | 45 flexures of ~32 nm span placed in plan, with their 45 apertures, on a body the size of the tile | A8.2 | **DONE** (iteration 6) — claim `C-0041`, challenge `CH-0055`. **It does not, at EITHER stroke, and it does not fail on area.** The cheap bound is 2.59× the footprint with the standoff feet — exactly the size that invites *"stack it in three levels"*, and `C-0017`'s envelope has room for three (`ℓ = 5.78 / 7.82 / 9.86 nm`). **Stacking buys nothing**: a standoff runs from the superstructure to its own beam plane and a tie from that plane to the tile, so any two vertical members share a height range whatever levels their beams sit at — the clash is **level-independent**. Two lattice facts meet nowhere: the grid's **across-helix pitch IS one duplex**, so beams in adjacent rows are tangent at zero tilt and mutually bury each other's ties at any other; and its **along-helix pitch, 13.33 nm, is under the span plus a duplex, 34.51 nm**, so a row's beams bury each other's standoff **feet**. **0 of 720 orientations, at any level count, on any body** — for 3 × 15 and for 2 × 15. The tile carries **exactly fifteen**: 1 × 15, span **21.44 nm = 63 bp**, feasible at **exactly one** of 720 orientations and that one supplied by the sheet's own helix direction. **The two clauses differ in KIND** — at 3 nm the binding variable is the **path count** (45 → 15, free: 6.67 pN/path against 10 pN, 2.16× buckling margin, tangent 25.49 pN/nm); at 10 nm the count is bounded **below at 29** by the same allowable and **above at 15** by the packing, so the window is **empty** and the binding variable is the **footprint**: **≥ 2330 nm², 1.44×, 1.20× in edge**. And the tie apertures are not 45 holes but `m` collinear slots that **sever** the superstructure into **18 components** at every one of the 32 phases — cured by **8 bp of stagger**. |
| T-101 | Is a 15-attachment scheme flat under the SOLVED load? | The dishing of a 1 × 15 grid under `C-0022`'s solved profile, against `T-5b`'s 10 % convention | A8.2 | **DONE** (iteration 7) — claim `C-0047`, raises `CH-0060`. **NO, and it is not `CH-0034`'s saturation — it is a LOSS.** 1 × 15 dishes **0.695 of the stroke**, **7.0×** `T-5b`'s convention, against 0.218 at 3 × 15 and 0.223 at 8 × 8; the criterion does not saturate below 45, and 15 sits **4.7× above** `CH-0034`'s 0.149 floor. **Below three columns a coupling is a NET DISHING SOURCE** — the free, uncoupled tile dishes 0.308, so 1 × 15 is **2.26× worse than no coupling at all**, break-even at exactly the three columns `C-0041` shows cannot be built. The **cheap bound** saw it in four operations: the along-helix Winkler bending length is **12.83 nm** against a 40 nm column pitch (3.12) and a 2.69 nm row pitch against 5.71 nm across (0.47). Orientation is worth 1.08× in dishing and **16× on the crossover path**, and the packing forces the good angle. The 8 bp stagger costs +2.19 % of the dishing and **breaks `C-0015`'s exact zero at first order** (0.389 pN, `CH-0060`); swept as a *design variable* it buys **45 %** back at ±13.60 nm — the bending length to 6 % — and still does not reach the tolerance. `C-0022`'s 32.1 % split is **unaffected**; what moves is the sag term, 32 % of the dishing at 3 × 15 → **79 %** at 1 × 15. |
| T-113 | Can a NON-UNIFORM coupling stiffness buy back the edge dishing? | The dishing of a 1 × 15 and a 3 × 15 grid with the rim springs stiffened, against `T-5b`'s 10 % convention | A8.2 | **DONE** (iteration 9) — claim [`C-0058`](gpd/claims/C-0058-non-uniform-coupling.md), raises [`CH-0071`](gpd/challenges/CH-0071-the-saturation-floor-is-a-property-of-the-equal-spring-family.md) against `CH-0034`. **YES at three columns, NO at one, and it is the first time anything in this programme has made the Gen-1 tile flat.** At 45 paths, redistributing `C-0017`'s **same** 33.3333 pN/nm — no extra stiffness, no extra attachments — takes the dishing from **0.2182** of the stroke to **0.0753** under a one-parameter rule (*the 34 stations within 6.7 nm of an edge carry 5× the other 11*) and **0.0544** under a 45-parameter optimisation, both inside `T-5b`'s 0.10, where `CH-0034`'s **count** axis saturates at 0.149. So **0.149 is the saturation of the EQUAL-SPRING family, not a rim floor** (`CH-0071`). **The cost is a force and it is affordable at 45 paths**: 2.762 pN per path at the 3 nm stroke against the 10 pN unzip allowable (3.62× clear), 0.784 pN in the worst crossover, 1.13 pN of duplex shear, and +24 % on `C-0014`'s thermal force — which is **linear** in a path's share, not its square root. **The per-path ceiling costs nothing at 45 paths** (capped and uncapped optima coincide) and binds at 15. **At `C-0041`'s 1 × 15 the axis fails**: 13.0 % admissible improvement, still 6.0× the tolerance and **1.96× worse than no coupling at all** — fifteen springs on one line can reweight a placement, not move it. **But flatness bought this way is owed at ONE state**: the same design is flat at three of `C-0022`'s five solved states and dishes 0.187 at the 2 nm gap where the *uniform* coupling dishes 0.071, and a minimax over all five reaches only 0.1587. Two smaller results: the break-even moves from three columns to **two**, and the lattice/plate excess **changes sign with the distribution** (−8 % uniform, +24 % at the flat design). Opens `T-122` (is a 5:1 per-path stiffness ratio buildable?) and `T-123` (is any distribution flat at every state?). ~~TODO — **medium-high, and it is the last unexplored axis `C-0047` leaves.**~~ Every attachment in the corpus is an *equal* spring: `C-0017`'s mandate fixes the **total** 33.3333 pN/nm and nothing upstream requires it to be shared equally. `C-0047` shows the dishing at fifteen paths is 79 % the coupling's own sag and that `C-0022`'s rim collar is where the load actually is, so stiffening the two rim rows (or the rim *stations* of a multi-column grid) at constant total is free of every standing claim and has never been run. The cheap bound is that the load is 1.3–1.9× the interior value inside an 8.9 nm collar, so the matched stiffness distribution is knowable before the solve. **It cannot rescue 1 × 15** — a single column has no rim stations along `x` — **but it is the only remaining way to attack `CH-0034`'s 0.149 floor**, which no attachment *count* touches. |
