| T-106 | The truss cap as a solved body, not a series spring | The cap's own geometry and rotational compliance, and `C-0037`'s frame couple recomputed on it | A8.2 | **DONE** (iteration 7) — claim `C-0048`, raises `CH-0061`. **The cap cannot be the flexure and cannot be a spring.** `C-0042`'s own two functions settle the geometry before any solve: the steric floor puts two legs `≥ 2R` apart and a leg is seated only within `R` of a seat's axis, so a duplex laid ACROSS the row seats **neither** (line contact **0.000 nm** at 6–16 bp, against 2.000 along it) — and the counting theorem says it again, one link per leg. **The cap is a separate crossbar duplex, 13 bp = 4.38 nm, hosting THREE 90° junctions, six links, its axis one radius above the leg heads.** Its *bending* is the small term (`12EI/w`, 8.93× the couple, so the frame couple falls only 74.18 → **71.31** pN·nm/rad at 7 bp — but 42.5 % at 16 bp, because it goes as `1/w` where the couple goes as `w²`); its torsion 0.1 %; its **height** raises `C12` and shortens the legs 1 nm. **What moves the answer is that `C-0037` applies `C-0029`'s counting theorem to the head's AXIAL path and takes its ROTATION as infinite** — 78.24 / 13.53 on a conserved 91.76, a **ceiling** on the head restraint, not a series correction. So the cap junction's azimuth is a **third** instance of one rank-one budget and, unlike the leg row's, it has **no free corner**: laid along the flexure axis the free plane caps at 6.20 pN and **no** separation to 16 bp crosses; laid across it, **7 bp** crosses again. **`C-0042`'s seven base pairs stands and its reason does not — the crossing is bought at the cap, not at the row.** All nine predicates PASS over `h = 5–10 nm` on both rigidities, at **1.95 / 1.46** against `C-0037`'s 2.79 / 2.10: the cap costs **30 %** of the margin, 38 % of the draw-in supply and 35 % of the tangent headroom, and `k_s`/32 now fails on **both** rigidities. Opens `T-109` (three junctions on one crossbar). |
| T-117 | Do THREE 90° junctions close on one crossbar duplex? | `C-0042`'s closure search at the other end of the same legs: two leg heads from below and the flexure's own end from the side, on a **lone** 13 bp seat with no lattice neighbours | A8.2 | **DONE** (iteration 8) — claim `C-0052`, raises `CH-0067`. **THEY CLOSE**, at `C-0048`'s own 13 bp and at every length to 19 bp and every row from 6 to 12: six links inside the measured `[0.60, 0.70]` nm step, **zero unpaired nucleotides**, six distinct targets, seat contacts 1.83–1.96 nm against `C-0042`'s 1.60 floor, and the wanted chord alignment at **0.00°** on the converged grid — and it still closes at **14 of 15** integer leg lengths with the legs' azimuths *carried up from `C-0042`'s base* rather than searched. Two cheap bounds were not formalities: the axial **rim** leaves exactly **0.02 nm** per end at every row width (`2R` is 5.88 rises and `ceil` buys 6 — and *"13 bp = 4.38 nm"* is a demand and a length, 13 bp being 4.42), and the **flexure's end passes a leg's surface at 0.249 nm**, tighter than the 0.54–0.69 nm gaps packed origami keeps, with the row width the only lever (0.184 at 6 bp, 0.714 at 12). **The finding is that a LEG IS ONE BODY WITH TWO JUNCTIONS**: its base and cap chords differ by `m × 33.74°`, so `C-0048`'s recommended 7.00 nm leg (21 steps) is **78.5°** from the azimuth pair its design is written on — and on the wrong side at 10.67, 10.5 **and** 10.4 bp/turn, with 13 steps the only length surviving all three. Only the *difference* is quantised (rotating the leg trades the two misalignments one for one), so **every** quantised length still passes all nine predicates at **1.81–2.43 / 1.36–1.83** — and at 21 steps the forced pair is **better** than the recommended one (2.20/1.66 against 1.95/1.46), because `C-0048` chose its cap azimuth on the **free** plane while the **loaded** one governs. The crossbar is a duplex: **31 `k_BT`** sequence-averaged and **17** at the worst sequence at 2 mM MgCl₂ on Huguet et al.'s measured magnesium nearest-neighbour set, and overhang is mechanically free because `12EI/w` and `4C/w` carry the **row**. |
| T-107 | Is `C-0023`'s 40 pN/nm compliance ceiling required at the DESIRED stroke, or only at the working point? | The ceiling re-derived from `C-0017`/`C-0018` with the stroke it is read at named | A8.2 | **DONE** (iteration 7) — claim `C-0049`, challenge `CH-0062`. **It is owed at the PLACEMENT stroke, 3 nm, and nowhere else** — 40 is exactly `1.2 x (100 pN / 3 nm)`, a **declared linearity tolerance on the placement discharge** that carries its stroke inside it, and the *same* construction at §3's desired clause is **12 pN/nm, not 40**. **Neither `C-0017` nor `C-0018` contains an upper bound on a coupling tangent**: placement is an EQUALITY on the secant, stability a FLOOR on the tangent, and `C-0032` measures a stiffer tangent *raising* `C-0018`'s pull-in margin. What binds beyond the working point is `C-0006`'s **per-path unzip allowable**, a bound on a FORCE, so as a stiffness it is `n·allowable/s` and **tightens as 1/s**: 150 → 45 pN/nm at 45 paths, 50 → **15** at `C-0041`'s buildable 15. `C-0039`'s `E5a16` secant at 10 nm (69.94) is past both, so the miss moves from **6.6x on a declared tolerance to 1.55x on a cited allowable** and **no verdict moves**. **And the same answer settles `CH-0047`/`T-76b`**: a device placed at 3 nm traverses `[0, 3]` and never occupies the 4.556 nm stroke `CH-0042`'s minimum is taken at — read at the placement stroke `C-0030`'s flexure is 25.227 pN/nm and clears **4 of `C-0017`'s 6** 2 mM floors, where 22.875 clears **none**. |
| T-108 | Is §3's DESIRED 10 nm stroke reachable by any coupling this programme has? | A synthesis over every element in the catalogue, or the plain statement that 10 nm is out of reach and 3 nm is not | A8.2 | **DONE** (iteration 7) — claim `C-0050`. **NO, and the bound that settles it contains no coupling at all.** The stroke is `s = L₀ − h`, so **`s < L₀` identically**, and §3 names no layer taller than 10 nm: a 10 nm stroke on a 10 nm layer IS `h = 0`. Best over 66 states — **kinematic** `L₀ − Nσv₀` **9.790 nm** (1.02x short), **validity** `L₀ − Nσv₀/0.2` (`C-0002`'s crossover, which is `C-0018`'s OWN binding bias ceiling at 10 nm) **8.959 nm** (1.12x), **dead-load at §3's 100 pN 7.424 nm** (1.35x) — and a coupling can only *reduce* the last, because `C-0017`'s own gate-2 theorem makes the delivered stroke monotone decreasing in the coupling stiffness. So the free stroke is the supremum over **every** coupling, and `T-108` is not a search. Catalogue: **0 of 14 rows clear at the desired stroke, 3 of 14 at the acceptable one** (`E3a` at 15 paths, `C-0023`'s linear `E5`, and **`E5a1`**). The telling row is `E5`, which clears every **coupling-side** predicate at 10 nm and fails on the reach. **Established: unreachable ON §3's OWN STACK** — stronger than *with this catalogue*, weaker than *in physics*. The escape is a **taller layer, 16.6–26.1 nm** → `T-115`. §6 task 3 is **unaffected**: its predicate is the ≥ 3 nm one. |
| T-118 | Re-synthesise the design window and the standing findings against iterations 5–7 | `C-0027`'s window and verdicts re-run against `C-0031`–`C-0050`, or a statement that no edge moves — with the **axis** each new constraint lives on named | A2.1 | **DONE** (iteration 8) — claim `C-0051`, raises `CH-0063` and `CH-0064`. **NOT ONE WINDOW EDGE MOVES** — 0 of 6, 0 grid steps, no owner change — and the reason is the finding: of the twenty claims of iterations 5–7 **exactly one** (`C-0036`) carries a quantity that is a function of `σ` at all, and it says itself it reaches the design only through `C-0018`'s bias ceiling. `C-0050`'s two NEW `σ`-resolved ceilings — kinematic `L₀ − Nσv₀` and validity `L₀ − Nσv₀/φ_c` — **could** narrow and do not, by 1.71–3.11× at all 61 grid points of both windows. **An axis LEAVES the acceptance stack** (`C-0049` withdraws `C-0023`'s 40 pN/nm, which is `1.2 ×` a *placement* mandate) and a **path count** replaces it. **The one thing that moved a verdict is a composition nobody had made**: `C-0033`'s collar (+2.61 to +4.99 pN/nm) and `C-0032`'s realised softening (−9.21 to −10.29) both move `C-0018`'s 10 nm / 2 mM fold and neither carries the other — composed exactly at `C-0018`'s own fold, where the baseline tangent vanishes by construction, the total is **−8.40 to −11.06 pN/nm at 6 of 6 models**, so the collar recovers **27–49 %** of what the element costs and `C-0032`'s **1.0000–1.0019** is the standing statement (`CH-0063`). And `C-0050`'s bound 3 is read on a layer **4.15× denser** than the window's own at its upper edge (`CH-0064`). **The deliverable is no longer a window**: §3's acceptable clause is delivered, its desired one is refused kinematically, and what is left is a **height plus five specification questions** of which only `T-115` can buy the desired stroke. |
| T-115 | May the polymer layer be taller than 10 nm — 17–26 nm? | A specification answer from NDI, not a calculation | A2.1 | **ANSWERED** (2026-08-18, NDI — decision 2) — **NOT EXAMINED, AND IT IS THE SAME RESERVE AS `T-63`.** *"17-26 nm of polymer thickness is beyond the regime I've bothered to examine as the debye length of operation in 2 mM MgCl2 is only about 4 nm … an interesting regime we've been reserving, again, for low MgCl2 concentrations we'd buy with additional work on stabilizing DNA origami at low salt."* So a tall layer is available **at 0.5 mM**, and buying it is the same purchase as `T-63`. The objection given — 4.3–6.6 bulk Debye lengths of separation — has never been answered here: no claim has evaluated the **bias** that delivers §3's 100 pN across a 17–26 nm gap. That is `T-192`, and **it has run: `C-0110` answers the objection and UPHOLDS it** — §3's 100 pN stops arriving across a **13.6989 nm** gap at 0.5 mM, so a 17–26 nm layer reaches it at 0 of 12 cells, admits device B at 1 of 96 states and refuses §3's *acceptable* clause at 96 of 96. The escape is real in displacement and empty in force (`CH-0127`), and a 17–26 nm layer also breaks §3's own effort-point row by 1.08–1.64× before any field is solved (`CH-0126`). ~~TODO — **it is a question, not a task, and it is the FOURTH this programme has raised** (after `T-95`, `T-102` and `T-63`), and the only one that can buy §3's **desired** stroke. `C-0050` shows the stroke is the layer's own compression, so `s < L₀ ≤ 10 nm` on §3's stated heights and the desired clause asks for `h = 0`. The layer height at which the 100 pN dead-load stroke reaches 10 nm is **16.63–26.12 nm** over `C-0003`'s six models at `σ` = 0.024 nm⁻². §3's own tile row already allows the effort point to sit *"~20–25 nm above the electrode"*, so the geometry is not absurd — but a 17–26 nm layer moves `C-0002`'s crossover, `C-0005`'s screening validity, `C-0007`'s drainage and `C-0012`'s bias, and **nothing in this programme has evaluated one**. **Carried to the open questions for Kazik.**~~ |
| T-116 | Does a 45-arm hinge-line array have a plan view? | 45 `E5a1` arms of 9.13 nm placed in plan on a 40 x 40 nm tile, with their 45 crossovers, or the statement that it does not pack | A8.2 | **DONE** (iteration 8) — claim `C-0053`, raises `CH-0065`. **NO — and `C-0041`'s answer does not transfer, so the reason is new.** Both of `C-0041`'s obstructions **dissolve**: `E5a1` owns **no vertical member** (0 blocking pairs, 0 clashes, at every orientation) and `arm + d = 11.82 nm` sits **under** the 13.33 nm column pitch where `C-0041`'s 34.51 sat 2.59× over it. As **bodies** the 45 arms pack — 0.685 of the footprint, 2 of 720 orientations, exactly `θ = 0` and `θ = π` (a rooted arm has a direction; `C-0041`'s centred beam does not). **What refuses them is the hinge lattice**: a hinge is a crossover, a row can only root on its **two bounding interfaces**, so roots sit at **5.44 nm** interior and **10.88 nm** on an edge row against an arm needing **11.82** — three arms per interior row, two per edge row, **43 of 45**, and 43 is **exact** (the construction meets the independent per-row bound). The best phases are `C-0015`'s **ten centro-symmetric** ones, for the third time. **And the two missing arms are the least of it**: an arm is *cut out of* the host, so 43 arms take **65.4 %** of its duplex length, spend **43** crossovers and bury **13** more — **56 of 56, exactly 100 %** — leaving **zero** surviving crossovers and **no bonded component at all**. Self-consistent count **43**; the count that leaves the host whole is **25** (15 duplexes bonded at 25, 14 at 30, 8 at 35, 3 at 40, none at 42), so the threshold is **45 → 25, 1.80×**. 45 never place on **any** of five swept axes (the double-nick reading of the root gives 30). One thing improves: the lattice carries **three** columns, exactly `C-0047`'s break-even, so this array is **not** a net dishing source where `C-0041`'s 1 × 15 is 2.26× worse than nothing. |

Three legs across reach 3.52x; a **triangle** and a **rectangle** reach 16.4 and 25.9 pN and **fail anyway**, on `P8` and `P4`, because they put frame couple in the LOADED plane and the supply collapses to 0.95 and 0.66 of the demand. The literature's own *"set of double-helical spacers"* is **exactly two**, 39 bp each, **one covalent link per end** (Pumm et al.'s SI strand table, counted by hand here) — so the published rigidity is a **frame couple made of ball joints**, which is this claim's mechanism, already built. |
| T-67 | Does a 90° scaffold or staple routing between a sheet duplex and a normal standoff exist at all? | A concrete strand routing, or a statement that it does not exist and the branch must be re-planned | A8.2 | **DONE** (iteration 4) — claim `C-0029`, raises `CH-0039` and `CH-0040`. **It exists, and it is the base's arithmetic that fails, not its chemistry.** Both links close covalently at **0.600 nm** inside the measured `[0.60, 0.70] nm` step, **zero unpaired nucleotides**, optimum a **scaffold excursion** (out at strand 1 bp 9, back at bp 10, chord −87.8°). But a duplex END has **two strand termini**, so a base has at most **two links** and a lever arm ≤ **1.0 nm**: `C-0028`'s `B2` needs 1.345 nm and its 261.2 pN·nm/rad is **3.34× over the hard ceiling of 78.24**. Two links lie on a chord ⇒ **one restrained axis**, and about the chord only 13.53 = `B1`; **a column buckles about its softest axis, so `P6` fails at every length** and **the standoff branch closes at §3's desired stroke** (it stands at the acceptable 3 nm, ℓ = 5–10 nm). The **90° exit is not set by the routing** — the azimuth is, quantised at 33.74°/bp and worth ≤ 8.4 %; the polar tilt is free, and one-sided sterics leave 30° of rocking. **`E5` as filed also fails, twice on geometry**: a `δ = r sin θ` law at **46.9°** (`t/s` = 1.549) and an arm capped by the mandate at **9.77 nm < 10 nm** at any hinge count. **What survives is `E5g16` — a 12.24 nm = 36 bp GUIDED arm on 16 crossovers**, tangent 33.68/38.68 at 3/10 nm, 2.04 pN per crossover, **no member in compression and no 90° junction anywhere**. |
| T-70 | What holds `E5g16`'s guided arm, and does `c = 12` survive its own anchorage? | The second anchorage's rotational compliance, and the arm cap `(c n EI/k)^(1/3)` recomputed on the realised `c` | A8.2 | **DONE** (iteration 5) — claim `C-0034`, raises `CH-0044`. **`c = 12` does NOT survive — the realised factor is 6.28–7.36 — and the cap does not need it to.** The end condition is a continuum, **`c(ρ) = 12(1+ρ)/(4+ρ)`**, exactly 3 at a free far end and 12 at a guided one and exactly **6 at `ρ = 2`**; and because **`ρ_far = k_far r/EI` carries the ARM**, the cap is a **fixed point**, `r = (c(k_far r/EI) n EI/k)^(1/3)`, so a longer arm buys its own guidance. **Every TWO-link anchorage clears §3's desired 10 nm stroke** — 13.43 nm at the arm's own duplex-end couple, 15.18 at a singly nicked continuation, 15.44 at a two-crossover clamp, and **10.97 nm even about the CHORD**, the axis the counting theorem leaves free (and which here is unloaded, because the arm carries no compression). **A ONE-link anchorage is exactly the cantilever, 9.77 nm, and fails** — and that is Rothemund's own observed failure. Design: **`E5a16`, an 11.03–12.50 nm = 32–37 bp arm on 16 crossovers**, tangent 33.56/36.78 at 3/10 nm, 1.83 pN per crossover, anchorage couple 16.7 pN per link = **7.3 bp** on `CH-0029`'s ladder. **The dominant compliance has changed sides: the ARM carries 58.5 %, not the hinge.** `CH-0044`: `c = 12` and `C-0023`'s series composition cannot both be right — the composition is exact **iff the far end carries no moment**, and `E5g16` read on its own `c = 12` is **1.64× over-placed**. |
| T-71 | A backbone-torsion check of `C-0029`'s closed routing | Whether the dihedrals of the scaffold-excursion junction close, not merely the phosphate distances | A8.2 | **DONE** (iteration 9) — claim `C-0057`, raises `CH-0070`. **They do NOT close at any of the three reported optima, and the reason is that the OBJECTIVE is blind, not that the junction is impossible.** `C-0029`'s single junction closes **0 of 4** links, `C-0042`'s pair **1 of 8**, `C-0052`'s trio **2 of 6**. Four of the eighteen are excluded by a **closed-form reach bound** — they close at *no* torsion: `C-0042`'s two 7 bp legs would need an `O3′–P` bond of **0.2517 / 0.2460 nm** against a covalent **0.16022 ± 0.00191**. The rest fail on **population**: `C-0029`'s binding link needs **ε = −22.9°** (0.015 % of 13 084 measured linkages) and its second **β = 27.4°** (**0 of 15 457** residues). **But a torsion-feasible placement EXISTS in `C-0029`'s own space and nobody was looking**: of **69 120** placements, 3 546 close on distance, **1 855** survive the reach bound and **18 of the 100 solved** close at torsion level — at gaps near **0.690 nm**, the *interior* of the window a residual-minimising search never visits. The scaffold excursion is **12.7× rarer** (280 covalent, 1 of 100 closing). Every constant is measured here from **876** X-ray DNA-only PDB entries and reproduces Parkinson's restraint library to ≤ 1.6° and Svozil's BI to ≤ 7.1°; the `[0.60, 0.70]` nm window this programme cites has **no primary source** and measures **0.607 / 0.664**. |
| T-72 | Re-price `T-66`'s triangulated standoff as a **stability** remedy, not a rigidity one | Whether a second standoff restores the free axis at an affordable cost in sway | A8.2 | **DONE** (iteration 6) — claim `C-0037`, raises `CH-0050`, **covers `T-66`**. **YES, and the trade does not exist in the form it was posed.** The draw-in lives on `(u_x, phi_y)` and the buckling that closed the branch lives on `(u_y, phi_x)`; a frame couple is a **rank-one tensor on the leg offsets**, so for a two-leg row at azimuth `theta`, `Sigma x^2 = (w^2/2)cos^2 theta` and `Sigma y^2 = (w^2/2)sin^2 theta` — **conserved, and at 90 degrees the loaded plane inherits EXACTLY ZERO**. Two legs laid **ACROSS** the flexure axis take the adopted critical load **1.46 -> 9.77 pN (6.71x)**, move the governing plane from free to loaded — restoring `C-0029`'s restrained-axis reading, which is exactly what `C-0029` said a truss would have to do — and cost **23 % of the draw-in supply** (2.90x the demand, against 3.75x) and **3.5 % of the tangent** (26.09 against a 40 pN/nm ceiling). **The window is `l = 5-10 nm`, all nine predicates, on BOTH rigidities**, where the single standoff fails `P6` at every length (0.40-0.57) and buckles at a 3.05-7.02 nm stroke. **Laid ALONG the axis the same two legs still fail `P6`** and lose 63 % of the supply — worse on every axis at once. **The cost is the leg COUNT, not the leg SPACING**: `L2a6`/`L2a8`/`L2a12` are bit-identical in the loaded plane. Unanticipated: **`P9`**, an off-square row reacts part of the head moment as an axial **couple** and overloads its outermost leg — it fails at **12 of 12** azimuths below 90 degrees, so the exact cross row is **uniquely** optimal. Design: **`L2a8` — two 8 nm = 24 bp legs at 8 bp = 2.72 nm across the flexure axis, span 33.43 nm = 98 bp, tangent 26.09 pN/nm, 180 standoffs = 45 % of a tile footprint**. |

paths to 5 / 75** — **4 to 9 over the entire bracket**, so the direction commits to nothing about the coupling. **The interlayer coupling turned out to be MEASURED and it closes a 39× bracket**: four bundles, two lattices, three laboratories, three techniques give `f` = **0.26–0.33**, and Wang et al. *publish* the rigid-composite formula, call it a *"naïve model"* of *"rigidly linked rods"* and measure it to over-predict by **2.7×** — `InterlayerCoupling.RIGID` is CanDo's assumption, in the very sentence both duplex constants come from. **One circular M13 pays for exactly four layers and not five**: 15 × 112 bp × 4 = **6 720** of **7 249** nt (92.7 %); five are 1 151 over. `C-0086`'s 4.31× excess is spent by the thickness §3 states, which is NDI's own arithmetic. **What survives is a statement about the COUPLING, narrowed 4.6×**: under `C-0087`'s measured dropout the best coupled four-layer 90th percentile is **0.116465044**, 1.16× the convention against the single layer's **0.532748246**, at 23.07 % exceedance against 100.00 % — and every coupled cell is *worse* than the uncoupled tile, which is `CLAUDE.md`'s *"an attachment coupling can be a NET DISHING SOURCE"* read on a tile that no longer needs the correction. 1.16× is inside what `C-0089`'s distribution (1.30–1.61×) and `C-0093`'s topology (2.22×) buy, **neither of which is searched here**. At the bracket's `COMPOSITE` end the coupled cells clear outright (0.0853679905 / 0.0920173317). **Three structural surprises**: `k_s/k_θ = S/B` makes the parallel-axis enhancement the SAME factor along and across the helices **exactly**, so a multi-layer sheet's anisotropy is **invariant** along the coupling axis (18.8735) and `C-0006`'s standing variant's **744.5** is a value the model cannot take at any coupling (`CH-0124`) — and it is not cosmetic, that variant reads 0.160153834 and would have called the four-layer tile *not flat*; `OrigamiGrillage` **never reads `layers`**, so the repository's lattice machinery is single-layer and square-lattice and the body had to enter as a smeared equivalent sheet; and `C-0093`'s four-layer brick is mis-specified in three ways whose net is **not signed** (`CH-0125`). **Eight upstream numbers reproduce, worst departure 7.2e−5**, including `C-0089`'s own 0.6142 and 0.532748246 and `C-0063`'s 0.3079 — the declared falsifier `F5` made the licence a test. Successors **`T-196`**, **`T-197`**, **`T-198`**. ~~TODO~~ |

| T-192 | **Device B in the corner all three answers point at: a 10 pN/nm coupling on a 17–26 nm layer at 0.5 mM** | `\|k_eff\|` and the pull-in fold at 17–26 nm in 0.5 and 2 mM against `C-0046`'s 10 pN/nm placement, plus the bias that delivers §3's 100 pN across such a gap | A8.2 | **DONE** — `C-0110`, raises `CH-0126` and `CH-0127`, both against `C-0050`. **NDI's objection is ANSWERED and it is UPHELD, and the answer is worse than the objection.** Measured on `C-0008`'s own solver, §3's 100 pN stops arriving across a gap of **13.6989 nm at 0.5 mM**, 11.8724 at 1 mM and 10.1299 at 2 mM — all **below** NDI's 17 nm floor, which begins 1.24× and ends 1.90× beyond where the 0.5 mM reserve leaves it. So 100 pN reaches a resting 17–26 nm gap at **0 of 12** (gap, buffer) cells; device B is admitted at **1 of 96** states and **1 of 6** layer models (a bracket disagreement, not a design); and §3's **acceptable** clause is refused at **96 of 96** — a 3 nm stroke from a 17–26 nm layer leaves a 14–23 nm gap. **The tall layer does not trade device A for device B, it loses both.** What it does buy is exactly what `C-0050` priced: the **uncoupled** tile reaches a 10 nm stroke at **52 of 96** states, so the escape is real in *displacement* and empty in *force*. Declared falsifier `F2` fired **adversely**: `ℓ/λ_D` is 0.910–0.983 at 2 mM (NDI's bulk `λ_D` is exactly right there) and **0.649–0.819 at 0.5 mM**, so the reserve makes NDI's own estimate optimistic. The counterion-dominance answer is true (6.37–38.94×) and is about ion CONTENT, not decay. Repaired an ulp overshoot in `EquilibriumPath.fold` that killed a nine-minute sweep; the repair moves no emitted number anywhere, by proof. |
| T-193 | **Template-stripped gold: the hold-down at the answered material, and where its potential of zero charge sits** | `C-0021`'s `M4` and `M3` re-read at gold alone rather than over four materials, and a literature value (or a documented absence) for the PZC / contact potential of template-stripped Au in mM MgCl₂ | A1.2 | **DONE** (iteration 23) — [`C-0111`](gpd/claims/C-0111-gold-electrode-pzc.md), raising [`CH-0128`](gpd/challenges/CH-0128-inverse-debye-length-called-with-a-bjerrum-length.md). **Half 1 confirms `C-0021` and moves nothing.** Gold is the *stiffest* of the four candidates, so the 2.6× bracket collapses onto its **adverse** end — 10.356–17.159 pN at 5 nm on a 2 nm tile, 0.737–1.422 at 10 nm — the state's own bracket narrowing by exactly **3.25905934×**; the deepest gold well anywhere in the box is **8.742 `k_BT`** against the 10 `k_BT` criterion and **0 of 6** gold states confine, because a `1/h³` force integrates to a bounded potential and naming a material changes the amplitude, never the exponent. One **ground** moves: `C-0021`'s retardation factor is no longer a *substitution*, though the DNA half is still retarded twice, so the low end stays a lower bound. **Half 2 answers `C-0021`'s open question 2, and the answer is adverse.** The model's *applied bias* is the **rational potential** `E − E_σ=0` — `V = ψ_d + σ_e/C_S` vanishes exactly when the electrode carries no free charge — so `C-0021`'s 0.9–5.1 mV table is a table of rational potentials, reproduced here from the opposite direction (diffuse drop in, bias out) at `3.6e−7` or better. Measured **`E_pzc(Au(111)) = 0.46–0.51 V vs SHE`** in 1 mM HClO₄ (Adnan, Behjati, Félez-Guerrero, Ojha & Koper, *PCCP* **26**:21419, 2024, open access, **read directly**, and the source's own RHE and SHE printings agree through Nernst at pH 3 to 1.6 mV), corroborated by Liu, Doblhoff-Dier & Koper, *ACS Electrochem.* **2**:995 (2026), *"around 0.5 V vs. SHE"*. That is **90.2–575.7×** the deciding threshold **and of the LIFTING sign**: an electrode at zero volt on that scale is negatively charged and repels the negatively charged tile. It also falls outside `C-0005`'s point-ion boundary at **9 of 9** states — 0.0974 V at a *negative* electrode against 0.1966 at a positive one — so **no force is quoted there**; the answer is the threshold, **the electrode must be held within 5.10 mV of its own PZC at the 10 nm layer and 0.886 mV at 5 nm**. **No `E_pzc` of gold in MgCl₂ or any divalent electrolyte exists**, on 32 recorded queries whose divalent leg is *not* empty (48 records, none of them a gold PZC). **Returns to NDI as one line** under decision 3: *is "zero bias" 0 V against a named reference electrode, the cell at open circuit, or two identical electrodes shorted?* — the three are hundreds of millivolts apart and differ in the **sign** of the resting force. |

| T-196 | **Where the four-layer tile stops being flat: the composite-fraction threshold** | The value of `f` at which `C-0109`'s UNCOUPLED four-layer free-tile dishing crosses `T-5b`'s 0.10, with its bracket and convergence — or the demonstration that the crossing is not a well-posed single number | A8.2 | **DONE** (iteration 24) — claim [`C-0116`](gpd/claims/C-0116-composite-fraction-threshold.md), task [`T-196`](gpd/tasks/T-196-composite-fraction-threshold.md), result `gpd/results/T-196-composite-fraction-threshold.json`, study `tile.CompositeFractionThresholdStudyKt`, model `tile/CompositeFractionThreshold.kt`, 10 tests written first and watched to fail. **`C-0109`'S OVERTURNING OF THE FLATNESS NEGATIVE SURVIVES, with 3.30× of margin on the ADVERSE end of the measured band.** The crossing is at **`f` = 0.0788618807** (bracket 0.0788618803…0.078861881), **monotone** — **one** sign change over all of `[0, 1]`, counted rather than assumed — against a measured interlayer coupling of **0.26–0.33**: the band's *low* end clears it by **3.29690337×** and its centre by **3.80411927×**. **Of three declared falsifiers NONE fired.** **The cheap bound removed the sweep before it ran**: `f` enters only through `1 + f(factor − 1)` and that one number multiplies `D_∥` *and* `D_⊥` alike (asserted at `1e-12`), so the threshold is a **scalar inversion**, not a 2-D search — `factor` = **39.4479652**. Four *independent* layers already dish only **0.18273857** against the single layer's 0.307902368, which is why `C-0109`'s interval was non-trivial. **`CH-0124`'s true honeycomb spacing has its OWN threshold** — factor **29.8359739**, crossing **0.105149174**, still cleared by **2.47267753×** — and both are carried because the challenge is open. Convergence: mesh **1.5e-05**, dishing grid **0.0**, bisection width **0.0**, scan resolution **0.0**. `C-0109`'s two numbers reproduce at **1.1e−10** and **8.6e−10**; result file **byte-identical across two independent JVM runs**. **What the verdict now rests on is one number nobody has measured**: the `f` a 15-wide × 4-deep **slab** realises, as against the measured **rods** — it would have to fall below **30 %** of the least-coupled measured bundle for the verdict to fail. Successors `T-197` (the coupled tile) and `T-198` (the honeycomb raster). |

| T-198 | **Can four honeycomb layers be RASTERED from one circular M13 at a buildable width?** | The admissible seamless row lengths of a **honeycomb** multilayer raster, and a routing that closes on 60 duplexes — or the statement that a seam is unavoidable and what it costs | A8.2 | TODO. `C-0109` answers the **count** (6 720 of 7 249 nt, 92.7 %) and explicitly not the **route**. `C-0086`'s odd-half-turn rule is a **square-lattice** statement, and applied at the honeycomb's 10.5 bp/turn it admits **no integer base-pair row length whatever** — an odd multiple of 5.25 bp is never an integer — so the rule is outside its own domain rather than prohibitive. Douglas et al. 2009 owns the honeycomb crossover rule (21 bp per interface, 7 bp planes, three azimuths); `C-0086`'s `P_D` tree theorem is about a single-layer row graph and a 4 × 15 stack is a **grid** graph, which has Hamiltonian paths. Also owed: **what the attachment lattice of a four-layer honeycomb top face is**, since every plan ceiling, phase and placement in this corpus is single-layer square-lattice. |

Add `-PbuildDirectory=<dir>` to any Gradle command when more than one agent is working this checkout (`P-7`),
and use [`tools/verify.sh`](tools/verify.sh) for an authoritative full-suite run — at four or more concurrent
agents the shared project lock defeats `-PbuildDirectory` on its own (`P-10`).
Run a study through [`tools/study.sh`](tools/study.sh) rather than `./gradlew study` whenever another agent is
working the checkout: `-PbuildDirectory` does **not** isolate `build-*/classes`, so a sibling build can delete
the classes out from under a multi-minute run (`P-12`).
Both scripts take `--drop <pkg>`, which removes a package another agent has left mid-TDD from the copy.

## Ordering rationale

NDI names Tasks 1 and 2 as the starting point, and the dependency structure agrees:
T-1 is the cheapest thing in the programme and T-3, T-4, T-7 and T-8 all consume its output.

T-8 is the next-cheapest — equipartition against the stiffness T-1 produces — and it is a *falsifier*:
a layer compliant enough to actuate may be too compliant to hold position at 300 K.
That tension is worth exposing early, so T-8 is promoted above its position in the problem definition's numbering.

P-3 and P-4 were raised as blockers rather than niceties because T-1's answer is only as good as its premises,
and the problem definition (§2, second caveat) says exactly that:
*"Where that crossover sits for our layer decides which exponent we are entitled to."*
That judgement was vindicated harder than expected: P-3 found the premise **violated**, not merely unchecked,
and the violation propagates into the chain length T-1 derives, not just into the exponent it applies.
Hence `T-1c` now sits above `T-2` for the same reason `P-3` sat above it before —
`T-2`'s deliverable is either a window or a *proof of emptiness*, and neither can rest on a failed premise.

## Standing findings that constrain everything downstream

- **Stiffness is not a single number at the resting height.** The SCF form has zero stiffness at first
  contact; the scaling form does not. Any downstream task quoting "the layer stiffness" must quote it
  at a stated compression. (`C-0001`, surprise S-1.)
- **The 10 nm design window exists, and so does a 7 nm one.** Under a *solved* density profile the 10 nm window
  is `σ ∈ [0.0116, 0.2601] nm⁻²` — **22.4× wide**, against strong stretching's 3.5× and the box models'
  *empty* — and 7 nm gives `[0.0296, 0.0496]`. **"Empty at 5 nm and 7 nm" is withdrawn**; it was carried from
  `C-0001` through three iterations. 5 nm is still empty and the ~10 nm desired stroke is still unreachable
  everywhere, which is `C-0001`'s one headline to survive a third independent model. (`C-0011`.)
- **The two profile models agreed with each other because they share a defect, not because they bracket an
  answer.** Neither the Alexander box nor strong stretching contains the chain's *entropic resistance to
  confinement*, and against an absorbing wall that term is not a correction — it is the **whole** disjoining
  pressure. Where both models say the tile floats free, the solved layer holds **78 pN**. (`C-0011`.)
- **The Gen-1 layer's height is a coil height, not a brush height.** `L₀ ∝ N^(0.49–0.64)`, not `N¹`, and
  `L₀ ≈ 1.6–2.9 R₀` everywhere — the resting height is set by the tail of a nearly-ideal coil, not by an
  osmotic balance. Every scaling height relation in the programme had assumed the opposite. And an SCF layer
  **has no resting height at all** unless one is defined: `P = 0` only asymptotically, so `L₀` is a convention,
  and a hundred-fold change in the defining load moves `N` by 2.5×. (`C-0011`, `CH-0010`.)
- **No part of the layer is semidilute — it sits *below* `φ#`, not above it.** `φ/φ# = 0.138` at the design
  point, peaking at 0.378. This reverses the direction `C-0003` asserted, and it puts the layer exactly where
  the fitted `αφ^(9/4)` limb is **least** constrained by data. (`C-0011`, `CH-0010`.)
- **The *bulk* solution is in the dilute→semidilute crossover, with `m_eff = 1.66–1.92` — but that is a
  bulk quantity and it does not transfer to the layer.** **The grafted layer's own osmotic exponent is
  2.00–2.56**, because the `φ lnφ/N` term that bends the bulk exponent below 9/4 is the translational
  entropy of whole chains, which grafting removes. So `m < 2` is what is excluded, not `m = 9/4`.
  (`C-0002`, `C-0003`, `CH-0002`.)
- ~~**`C-0001`'s strokes are lower bounds, and its window a lower bound on its own width.** Every
  correction found in P-3 makes the layer softer — exponent down, prefactor ×0.751, excluded volume ×0.230.~~
  **WITHDRAWN by `CH-0002`.** The direction is wrong. The height relation — which `CH-0001` had itself
  identified as the unrepairable part — is the larger effect and runs **stiffer**: stiffness at first
  contact is 33–87 % *higher*, not 19 % lower, and the stroke bracket **straddles** `C-0001` at every
  height. Concluding a direction from the corrections one happens to have is a distinct failure mode from
  getting a correction wrong, and this is an instance of it.
- **PEG in water is a *marginal* solvent, and the Gen-1 chains are not swollen at all.** The measured `A₂`
  gives an excluded volume of **12.25 Å³** against a 60.4 Å³ monomer, so the thermal blob is 1222 Kuhn
  segments — **167 kDa** — while the whole design space is 60–375 monomers, i.e. **0.02–0.10 of one blob**.
  Every blob-based statement about this layer was about a structure it does not have. (`C-0003`.)
- **The Alexander-de Gennes unity prefactor is worth 6.6× in excluded volume** — `L₀ = N a^(5/3)σ^(1/3)` is
  reproduced exactly by a two-body box layer at `v = 81.0 Å³`, against the measured 12.25 Å³. Quote it as a
  scaling, never as a number. (`C-0003`.)
- **The layer response is only weakly sensitive to the interaction strength**: `k ∝ K^(1/(m+1))` and
  `N ∝ K^(−1/(m+1))` exactly, because the chain length a specified height demands moves against the
  interaction. A **16×** change in `K` moves the stroke only from 5.81 to 4.38 nm. This bounds `P-9`'s
  exposure, and it says not to spend effort narrowing `Π_int` before checking this exponent. (`C-0003`.)
- **A compliant brush and a semidilute brush may be mutually exclusive for PEG.** Reaching the
  des Cloizeaux domain needs σ = 0.99 nm⁻² at 10 nm and 3.96 nm⁻² at 5 nm — the latter closer than one
  Kuhn diameter, so unrealisable; the former melt-like and ruled out by §4(a) for stiffness. (`C-0002`.)
- **The §2 chain-tension caveat is discharged.** 4.2 pN per chain at the design point against a ~30 pN
  threshold, and structurally incapable of reaching it by grafting density alone. (`C-0002`.)
- **§4(d) poroelasticity is discharged as a non-constraint, with its boundary named.** 91 kHz at the
  nominal design point, 22.6 kHz at the §3 worst case, 5.6 kHz under a composite worst case — against a
  1 kHz requirement. Drainage is a **footprint** problem, not a thickness problem (`τ ∝ L²`, `h` cancels),
  and a **denser** layer drains faster, so the binding direction is dilution. The design would have to
  leave the poroelastic model's own domain of validity before poroelasticity could bind. (`C-0004`.)
- **The buffer does not reach the layer's mechanics.** The mobile-ion channel is **exactly zero** by a
  conservation argument — ideal excluded salt gives a free energy strictly linear in φ, which `Π = φf′ − f`
  annihilates — despite carrying **3.5× the layer's own osmotic pressure** at 10 mM. The solvent-quality
  channel is ≤ 0.4 % of the modulus over 2–10 mM; it would take a salt 1.35× stronger than any in the PEO
  literature to reach 1 %. **But the layer-local Mg²⁺ is 33–66 mM, not 2–10 mM, and goes as `1/h`** — a
  ≤ 1.7 % stroke-dependent stiffness term, and the only positive-feedback term anywhere downstream. (`C-0007`.)
- **`χ` for PEG/water is 0.372 at 300 K, measured — not the 0.45 that was cited, which has no primary source
  at all** (the 0.44 in circulation is *polystyrene in toluene*). And `χ` carries a lattice-site convention
  worth a factor of **2.010**, the exact analogue of `C-0002`'s three meanings of `a`. (`C-0007`.)
- ~~**A bulk `χ` is not a brush `χ`, and that gap is 239× everything else in this section.**~~
  **CORRECTED by `C-0013`/`CH-0012`. A bulk `χ` and a brush `χ` differ by at most 0.053, and the 0.23 that was
  feared was a units error.** `χ ≈ 0.60` for a grafted PEO layer was `1.2 × ½` assembled from an abstract —
  the source's own fits are 0.789/0.852 on a scale whose theta is **0.696**, for an air/water Langmuir
  monolayer under *lateral* compression, and the paper forbids that transfer explicitly. Normal-compression
  osmotic stress on grafted PEG **denser** than the Gen-1 window gives `χ_eff = 0.346–0.424` against a bulk
  0.372 — worth −11.4 % to +4.3 % of the stiffness, a fifth of the model bracket already carried.
  **What remains open** is that no compression measurement exists *inside* the Gen-1 window, so the bound
  comes from above and assumes monotonicity in grafting density. (`C-0013`, `CH-0012`.)
- **Mean-field screening is uncontrolled across the whole working range, and qualitatively safe across it.**
  The deviation is 123–214 % for Mg²⁺ at 5–10 nm gaps, so PB is not merely inaccurate there but outside the
  control of its own expansion; yet correlation attraction needs a gap under 1.46 nm, which the layer never
  allows. Both edges of the boundary miss the working gap — one 3.4× below, one 1.3× above. **`Ξ ∝ q³`: the
  divalence does this, not the surface charge** (Na⁺ at the same surface gives `Ξ = 3.0` against 24). (`C-0005`.)
- ~~**Point-ion PB at the electrode dies above ~0.197 V of diffuse-layer drop — 10× below the §3 ≤ 2 V target.**~~
  **CORRECTED by `CH-0007`.** The 0.197 V is a **diffuse-layer drop**, not an applied bias, and the two differ
  by the compact-layer drop — which takes 66 % of 0.1 V and 88 % of 2 V, because the electrode charge is
  exponential in `ψ_d` while the compact term is linear in it. The boundary is therefore at **≈ 1.0 V of
  applied bias**, so §3's 2 V ceiling exceeds it by 1.2×, not by 10×. Comparing a `ψ_d` threshold against a
  §3 bias without the Stern series is the error. (`C-0005`, `C-0008`, `CH-0007`.)
- **The output coupling has two requirements and only one of them contains physics.** Stability fixes a lower
  bound; **placement** fixes the value, because the force delivered over a stroke is `k_c·Δs` — so §3's own
  100 pN and 3 nm give **33.333 pN/nm by arithmetic**. Placement is written on the coupling's **secant** and
  stability on its **tangent**, so a strain-stiffening element discharges both with one part and the
  tangent/secant ratio is free stability margin. **The stiffness was never the constraint** — 45 duplexes in
  tension are 148× too stiff; the design problem is spending stiffness as *compliance*, and the dominant
  compliance term is a 13 nt ssDNA spacer at 99.6 % of the load path. (`C-0017`, `CH-0016`.)
- **Normal stabilisation and lateral confinement want the *same* anchors, not opposite ones**, because the two
  requirements differ by **72.4×** (33.3 against 0.46 pN/nm). An anchor sized on the normal condition delivers
  lateral confinement with 70× to spare; one sized on the lateral condition delivers 0.4 % of the normal.
  (`C-0017`.)
- **Three of the five axes this programme discovered do not resolve in grafting density at all.** Flatness,
  the usable bias window and the output-coupling stiffness are height-level or topological, so they cannot
  *narrow* a window — only *close a height*. **A `(σ, L₀)` window is the wrong shape for the Gen-1 decision**,
  and a constraint that cannot narrow is invisible to an intersection. (`C-0016`.)
- **`L₀/R₀ ≥ 1` is exactly vacuous, not weak** — it admits all 183 points of the sweep, including `Σ = 0.063`
  at 22 nm spacing. Coil overlap owns the lower edge of every surviving window. (`C-0016`.)
- **The unzip exceedance `C-0015` found is unreachable inside the window** — the solved layer's foundation
  multiplier is 0.823–1.605 and `C-0015` entered the band only at ×0.25 of the foundation sweep. A loosening
  neither claim could see alone. (`C-0016`.)
- **Static stability wants the thin layer, whose window is empty; the window, the stroke and the force trade
  all want the thick one.** All three pull the same way and stability pulls against all three. **That
  inversion, not any single number, is the Gen-1 design problem.** (`C-0016`.)
- **The §6 task 3 target is reachable, and the operating point it is reachable at is not one the device can be
  held at.** 100 pN *at* a 3 nm stroke needs only 0.082–0.368 V — but at the loaded operating point
  **`k_eff < 0` at 7 nm and 10 nm**, so the §6 target requires an output coupling that supplies **5–72 pN/nm of
  its own stiffness**, comparable to the whole layer at first contact. And the *free* operating point leaves
  three upstream validity ranges at once above ~0.1 V, so **the usable bias window is 0.02–0.1 V, not 0–2 V**.
  (`C-0012`.)
- **The blocking force is not the peak output force — it understates it by up to 20×.** `dW/dh = k_eff`
  exactly, so wherever the field softens the layer the characteristic *rises* with stroke. (`C-0012`.)
- **`k_es` changes sign at 0.55–1.58 nm, and that is what arrests the collapse.** §6 task 4's second branch is
  nearly right, but the arresting mechanism is **electrostatic, not osmotic**. (`CH-0011`.)
- **The two halves of §6 task 3 run in opposite directions with layer height** — blocking force gets 10×
  harder from 5 to 10 nm while stroke gets 10× easier — so a single "bias needed" figure hides both.
  Static stability then opposes both: 5 nm is stable and 10 nm is not. (`C-0012`.)
- **100 pN at ≤ 2 V is reachable on the electrostatics alone, with room** — 0.067 V at 5 nm, 0.113 V at 7 nm,
  0.679 V at 10 nm in 2 mM buffer, all comfortably inside the point-ion boundary. But **the actuator is
  voltage-saturated above ~0.5 V**: a factor of 8 in bias buys 1.9× in force, so §3's 2 V ceiling is almost
  irrelevant to what the device can do. (`C-0008`.)
- **The force's decay length is a fourth number, and the only bias-dependent one** — 1.8–2.8 nm at the working
  gap, rising to the bulk `λ_D` in the far field, and `λ_D/2` at zero bias against `λ_D` under bias. None of
  `CH-0004`'s three lengths is it. (`C-0008`, resolving `CH-0004`.)
- **§4(c)'s sign reversal now carries a force: the layer *amplifies* `F_es` by 1.15–1.60×**, largest at 10 mM
  and under compression. And **finite ion size *raises* the force by up to 56 %**, so point-ion PB is a
  **lower** bound on `|F_es|`, not an upper one. (`C-0008`.)
- **§4(c) has the sign backwards: the polymer layer *protects* the field.** It admits only 52–77 % of the bulk
  salt, so the local Debye length is 1.14–1.39× **longer** inside the layer, and lengthens further under
  compression. The dielectric-decrement mechanism §4(c) names is 3.9 % at φ ≈ 0.03 — the layer is 97 % water.
  The bound is one-sided (exclusion only); cation coordination by PEG's ether oxygens could flip it (`P-8`). (`C-0005`.)
- **"The Debye length" is three different numbers here, and all three are right in their own place** — 3.93 nm
  in bulk buffer, 0.84–1.18 nm in the counterion-dominated gap, 4.5–5.5 nm inside the PEG layer. Substituting
  one for another is `CH-0004`. (`C-0005`.)
- **Lateral confinement is decided by anchor *orientation*, not material — and it puts a footprint constraint
  on the device that scales with the stroke.** For any flexible link crossing the layer, `k_lat/k_norm` is the
  secant over the tangent of its force-extension law, which convexity bounds at **≤ 1**: a through-layer path
  costs at least as much normal stiffness as it buys laterally. A rigid vertical strut is 40–160× worse still,
  costs 96–99 % of the stroke, and is **destabilised by the load it exists to resist**. What works is a path
  lying *in* the surface — but such a tether converts stroke into tension geometrically, so
  **`L_min = δ√(Sn/2A)`**: 28 nm of tether for §3's acceptable 3 nm stroke, **93 nm for the desired 10 nm**,
  i.e. a ~100 nm assembly around a 40 nm tile, rising to ~230 nm. **Lateral confinement and the desired stroke
  are incompatible at a fixed footprint — not in physics.** (`C-0014`.)
- **Over-stiffening an anchor is not free.** The per-anchor thermal force is `√(k_BT k)/N`, so a 120×-margin
  scheme puts 29 pN on a load path where the minimum design puts 2.6 pN — past the unzip allowable. (`C-0014`.)
- **The layer confines the tile in one direction only.** Its lateral restoring stiffness is **exactly zero by
  symmetry** — a laterally homogeneous grafted layer under a non-adsorbing tile has a translation-invariant
  free energy — so the tile diffuses 62.8 nm in one 1 kHz period, 21× the σ_RMS predicate. And it exerts **no
  upward force above `L₀`**, so at zero bias the tile is unconfined in *both* directions. Whatever holds it
  down is not in the §3 stack. (`C-0010`; `T-12`, `T-13`.)
- **A variance without a bandwidth is the `f → ∞` limit, and here that is 13× the in-band number.** Only
  0.55–3.07 % of the tile's variance lies below 1 kHz, so the in-band σ_RMS is 0.07–0.77 nm where the
  broadband figure is 0.87–4.38 nm — and the predicate passes in band even where it fails broadband. (`C-0010`.)
- **The stroke and the noise are set by different stiffnesses, and the gap is a factor of three.** The secant
  (16.6–26.1 pN/nm) sets the stroke; the tangent at the working point (47.7–64.1) sets the fluctuation.
  Substituting one for the other is a 1.6× error in amplitude. Relatedly, **actuating the tile quiets it** —
  the piston RMS falls 4.2× from unbiased to the working point — but that is one-sided, because `k_es < 0`
  runs the other way. (`C-0010`.)
- **The tile is not a rigid plate.** `ℓ/L = 0.14–0.64`; it is rigid only for a perfectly uniform load,
  where it is rigid *exactly*, whatever its rigidity. Any concentrated coupling, discrete anchor, load
  non-uniformity or thermal excitation dishes it by 26–369 % of the stroke. A point-coupled lever and an
  area-averaging charge sensor therefore **do not measure the same displacement** — they differ by 26 % of
  the stroke. §4(g)'s own test for abandoning the rigid-plate picture is met. (`C-0006`, `CH-0005`.)
- **The output coupling must be near-continuous, but the count is 45 and the "more attachments than load
  paths" reading is withdrawn.** Searched over grid *shapes* rather than counts — the sheet is 25.6× stiffer
  along the helices than across them, so the square diagonal was the wrong slice — flatness needs **45
  attachments as 3 × 15**, i.e. **0.80 per crossover**, not `C-0009`'s 64 as 8 × 8 and not `C-0006`'s 55.
  Stronger: **one attachment row per duplex zeroes the peak per-load-path force exactly**, by the same
  symmetry that makes a uniform load dish nothing — and it is just as fragile, since any load non-uniformity
  restores it in proportion. (`C-0006`, `C-0009`, `C-0015`, `CH-0014`.)
- **A load-path contour average is not a peak.** A rigid anchor is carried by its **two nearest crossovers and
  essentially nothing else**, so the equal-share figure understates the peak by **2.3–7.6×**. The worst case
  reaches the 10–15 pN unzip allowable — but `C-0015` shows **layout nearly closes it**: at the design point
  every layout is clear of the band, and at the soft end the best registration removes 4.60 pN of a 4.65 pN
  excursion, landing 0.5 % inside. The lever is the attachment's **distance to the nearest crossover**
  (×1.43–1.60), not the crossover count, whose effect is 0.3–3.4 % **with the opposite sign** to `C-0009`'s.
  For a *concentrated* coupling, layout alone decides whether one attachment sits above or below the 48 pN
  duplex-shear allowable. (`C-0009`, `C-0015`.)
- **A discretisation is not automatically a relaxation.** The continuum plate errs in **both** directions and
  the sign is set by how the load meets the sheet, not by the discreteness: softer under a point load
  *entering* it, stiffer under a point *reaction* and a smooth taper, because a 2 nm duplex cannot bend across
  its own diameter and the continuum lets it. `C-0006` asserted the softening direction universally.
  (`C-0009`, `CH-0008`.)
- **The 35–60 pN band is not a per-load-path allowable.** It is a *whole-cross-section* disassembly force for
  a 6–8-helix tube at 5.5 pN/s, and a DNA rupture force without a loading rate is not a material constant.
  Per path, use single-duplex shear (~48–65 pN) or unzip (10–15 pN), with 65 pN a hard ceiling. (`C-0006`.)
- **The layer is ~1.5 blobs tall.** `L₀/s = (Σ/π)^(5/6)` identically, so the conventional `Σ = 5` onset
  buys 1.47 blobs and a ten-blob stack needs `Σ ≈ 50`. This is a *geometric* failure of the same
  convention `CH-0001` failed thermodynamically, and the two are inverse powers of the same `Σ`.
  Strong-stretching theory is also outside its own premise here: `L₀/R_F = 1.17–1.25`. (`CH-0003`.)

## Open questions for Kazik

> **ANSWERED — 2026-08-18.** Kazik put the six collected in
> [`DECISIONS-FOR-NDI.md`](DECISIONS-FOR-NDI.md) to Jeremy Barton and all six came back in one pass.
> Items 2, 3, 8, 9 and 10 below are the five of them that live in this list, and each now carries
> its answer at the top; item 11 records the sixth, which was in the decision file and never in this
> list — a drift of the kind item 6 and item 7 record in the other direction.
> **The analyses are kept as written and struck nowhere they are still true**, per the same discipline.
> The work the answers open is `T-191`–`T-195`; the answers are recorded in
> [`JOURNAL.md`](JOURNAL.md).

**1. Two paywalled PDFs would close the only genuinely missing measurements in `P-6` and `P-8`.**
This is an **access** limit, not a compute limit — ACS, Elsevier, Springer and IOP all refuse an automated fetch,
and Crossref/EuropePMC serve only the abstracts (which is how the bound in `C-0007` was built at all).

- **Boucher & Hines, *J. Polym. Sci. Polym. Phys. Ed.* 14:2241 (1976)** — the one study that measured Group II
  chlorides against PEO, and so the only source for the θ-versus-[MgCl₂] curve *including the minima* that make
  a linear salting-out coefficient ill-posed in the first place. Paywalled and pre-digital; only the abstract
  is reachable, via Crossref.
- ~~**Lee et al., *J. Phys. Chem. B* 116:7367 (2012)**~~ — **STRUCK. The body was obtained free**, from NIST's
  public repository, because two coauthors are NIST staff. Worth recording as a general lesson: **Unpaywall
  and OpenAlex both report this paper `closed` and both are wrong.** Check the authors' institutions for
  federal or institutional repositories before declaring a body unreachable.
- Any PEG/PEO salt study **below 50 mM**. `C-0007` found none at all — every cloud-point and aqueous-two-phase
  paper works at 0.1–3 M, which is two orders of magnitude above the Gen-1 buffer and exactly where a
  non-monotonic `θ(c)` would have its structure. This may simply not exist rather than be paywalled.

Neither is blocking: `C-0007` bounds the effect they would pin at ≤ 0.4 % of the layer modulus, and states the
bound as a falsifiable threshold rather than a guess. They would convert a bound into a number.

**2. What is the electrode made of, and where is its potential of zero charge?**
**ANSWERED, 2026-08-18 — TEMPLATE-STRIPPED GOLD**, *"for initial experiments"*.
The material half is settled and it collapses the 2.6× bracket onto its **adverse** end — `C-0021`'s gold row
becomes the number rather than the top of a range — while **no verdict moves**, because the finding there is
that a `1/h³` force integrates to a *bounded* potential.
**The potential of zero charge was not given**, and that is the half that carries the entire thermal-scale
hold-down (0.9–5.1 mV of contact potential does all of it). Re-queued as `T-193`; if the literature does not
pin a template-stripped Au PZC in mM MgCl₂, it returns to NDI as a one-line ask.
**`T-193` IS DONE (iteration 23, `C-0111`) AND IT DOES RETURN AS A ONE-LINE ASK — but not the one this row
expected.** The literature *does* pin the number, for Au(111) in 1 mM HClO₄: **`E_pzc` = 0.46–0.51 V vs SHE**
(Adnan et al., *PCCP* **26**:21419, 2024, read directly). What is missing is not the electrode's number but
the **cell's**: the model's *applied bias* is the **rational potential** `E − E_pzc`, so the 0.9–5.1 mV table
is a table of rational potentials, and 0.46–0.51 V is **90.2–575.7×** it **and of the LIFTING sign**.
**The one-line ask is therefore about the drive, not the material:** *is "zero bias" 0 V against a named
reference electrode, the cell at open circuit, or two identical electrodes shorted?* The three are hundreds
of millivolts apart and they differ in the **sign** of the force at rest. No `E_pzc` of gold in MgCl₂ exists
(32 recorded queries), and that gap is bounded rather than open — the Au(111)/Au(110) facet spread is 0.3 V,
where 0.46 V would be needed to reach the deciding scale.
Raised by `C-0021` as `P-13`, and it is a **specification** gap rather than a modelling one, so no amount of
calculation closes it.
§1 says *"patterned electrode"* and never says of what.
Metal against oxide is **2.6×** on the van der Waals hold-down — the one term no design can remove — which is
larger than the DNA Hamaker constant (1.17× after the square root), larger than retardation, and larger than
the polymer in the gap.
And zero *applied* bias is not zero *charge*: a contact potential of **0.9–5.1 mV**, below anything a bench
would call zero, supplies the **entire** thermal-scale hold-down by itself.
A better calculation of the wrong material is not an improvement, so the answer is bracketed and handed back
rather than chosen.

**3. Should Gen-1 be specified at 0.5 mM MgCl₂ rather than §3's 2 mM?**
**ANSWERED, 2026-08-18 — PRICED, NOT GRANTED.**
*"Concerningly below the typical experimental stability window of DNA origami. There are routes to stabilize
against that kind of concentration, but's pushing a parameter hard that I've been reserving for additional
operating margin. So… well identified."*
So **2 mM stays the nominal**, 0.5 mM is a costed option rather than a recommendation, and **`T-50` is not
deleted** — both this list and the decision file predicted that a one-word answer would delete it, and the
answer was a *price*, which was not one of the two words on offer.
**And it is the same reserve item 9 asks for**, so the two questions are one spend (`T-194`).
This is the **single largest open design decision in the programme**, it is a specification question rather
than a calculation — which is why it is here rather than in the queue (it is `T-63`) — and as of iteration 5
it has stopped being an improvement and become a **requirement**.
**Six independent routes recommend it**: `C-0012` on the force clause, `C-0016` on the bias window,
`C-0017` on the stability floor, `C-0018` on the usable bias, `C-0027` on the corrected margin,
and `C-0032` on the realised coupling law.
At 0.5 mM the pull-in fold **does not exist** (0 of 6 states, against 6 of 6 at 2 mM), and the corrected
stability margin is **2.16–9.87× against 1.23–1.53×** — the only margin in this repository that clears
`C-0005`'s own 123–214 % mean-field error.
~~It costs **nothing**~~ **— it costs nothing IN THE PHYSICS, which is not the axis the price was on
(2026-08-18)**: `C-0007` puts the layer's buffer sensitivity at ≤ 0.4 %, so the window edges, the chain
length, the stroke and the chemistry are all unchanged.

**What changed the character of the question is `C-0032`.**
Every earlier route made 0.5 mM the *comfortable* choice while 2 mM remained defensible.
`C-0017`'s free stability margin was banked on the coupling being strain-**stiffening**,
and `C-0030` has since established that the realised coupled-standoff flexure strain-**softens**
(`t/s` falls 1.095 → 0.757).
Re-running `C-0018`'s fold analysis on that law over 216 states,
the 10 nm / 2 mM bias margin collapses from 1.007–1.032 to **1.0000–1.0019** —
the device is placed *on* its own fold —
and the fold's stroke walks back from 3.41–4.13 nm to 2.80–3.17 nm,
**through §3's own 3 nm target at two of six layer models**.
At 0.5 mM every predicate clears, at 1.44–5.93× on stiffness and 1.038–2.327× on bias.
The obvious escape is priced and fails: the adverse mounting is 42.4–61.0 pN/nm,
past `C-0023`'s 40 pN/nm compliance ceiling at **0 of 8** lengths.
A third route — a shorter standoff — lands **2.2 % short** at ℓ = 5 nm and is excluded by the
10 pN unzip allowable at ℓ = 4 nm.

**The alternative is `T-50`, costed at 1–3 weeks of primitive-model Monte Carlo for a regime `C-0005` reports
has no systematic theory at all.**

**4. Two queued tasks now exceed what one session can run, and one of them is on the critical path.**

- **`T-50` — the beyond-mean-field treatment of the actuated gap — is the last unbounded exposure on the
  Gen-1 critical path**, and `CH-0019` has just established that nothing else in the queue can reach it.
  `C-0017`'s 10 nm coupling margin is 1.19–1.42× and sits inside `C-0005`'s 123–214 % **electrostatic**
  one-loop correction; `T-1f` bounded the **polymer** correction at ≤ 9.4 % and showed it is a different
  expansion acting on the other term of `k_eff`, so it does not help. `C-0005` costs primitive-model Monte
  Carlo at **1–3 weeks of wall clock** and records that `Ξ = 17–24` has no systematic theory at all.
  **There is a free alternative and it is already in the programme**: adopt leaf `A2.2`'s 0.5 mM operating
  point, where the same margin is 2.09–8.65× and clears its own uncertainty. The decision is whether Gen-1
  is specified at 0.5 mM — a design choice, not a calculation.
- **`T-9`** (the crossover hinge constant from oxDNA) is unchanged: 2–5 k nucleotides, µs-scale umbrella
  sampling on 8 cores, **days not weeks**. It fits the machine; it does not fit inside one session.
  Its urgency rose again this iteration — `C-0026` shows the crossover's *thermal* force is `√(k_BT k_v)`
  and **diverges** in the programme's own lattice, so it is bracketed at 2.78–115.8 pN and only `T-9`
  turns that into a number.

Both are flagged so the decision to start them is yours rather than made by accident.

**5. Nothing else has needed more compute than this box provides.** `T-9` (crossover hinge constant from oxDNA)
is the first queued item that would run for *days* rather than minutes — costed at 2–5 k nucleotides and
µs-scale umbrella sampling on 8 cores. It fits the machine; it does not fit inside one session.
Flagged so the decision to start it is yours rather than made by accident.

**6. May the output superstructure be perforated under each flexure midspan — 45 duplex-omission holes as a
floor, and 45 slots one and a half times the tile's own footprint if §3's *desired* 10 nm stroke is wanted?**
Raised by `C-0035` (`T-78`), and it is a **specification** question about a body §1 and §3 never describe,
so no amount of calculation closes it.
The favourable mounting — the only one of four that is buildable at all — requires the flexure's midspan tie
to cross the plane its own standoffs stand on.
So the superstructure needs **45 duplex-sized holes, 326 nm², 20.4 % of the tile footprint**, at every stroke;
that is the floor and it is not optional.
**The bracket:** an **imperforate** superstructure caps the stroke at `ℓ − 2.69 nm` — **5.31 nm** at the
recommended 8 nm standoff, which delivers §3's *acceptable* 3 nm clause and nothing more.
**The threshold:** §3's *desired* 10 nm stroke needs each hole widened into an **18.37 × 2.69 nm slot**,
totalling **2223 nm² — 1.39× the whole 40 × 40 nm tile footprint** — or a standoff of `ℓ ≥ 12.7 nm`, which is
outside `C-0017`'s envelope.
Either half is settled by one sentence about what the superstructure is.
It is `T-95` — and it is **DISCHARGED**, not open. `C-0071` (iteration 14) found it had stopped applying: it was raised by the flexure-and-tie branch that `CH-0081` removed from the output role, and **nobody noticed at the time**. Kept here with its analysis intact, struck rather than deleted, because *a list that only ever grows is not a record* — the same discipline [`DECISIONS-FOR-NDI.md`](DECISIONS-FOR-NDI.md) applies in its discharged table. **Do not carry it to NDI.**

**7. May §3's tile grow by 1.44× in area — 2330 nm² instead of 1600 — to buy the *desired* 10 nm stroke?**
Raised by `C-0041` (`T-96`), and like item 6 it is a **specification** question, not a calculation.
The flexure array's minimum body is `n(L + d)·d` exactly, and it carries **no aspect ratio**:
growing the tile across the helices adds rows, growing it along them adds columns, and the product is the same.
On the specified 40 × 40 nm tile the path count is bounded **below at 29** by the 10 pN unzip allowable at the
desired stroke and **above at 15** by the plan-view packing, so **the window is empty** — the two clauses of §3
differ in kind rather than in degree.
**The threshold:** a tile of **≥ 2330 nm², 1.44× the Gen-1 footprint and 1.20× in edge** — for instance
29.9 × 78.0 nm, or the same area at any other aspect.
**The price is favourable where it is usually feared:** `C-0022` finds a larger tile costs *less* at the rim,
**+6.3 %** instead of +14.7 %, because the fringing collar is a fixed 1.65 nm and scales as `1/L`.
It also relieves `T-99`'s hinge-line inventory in the same direction.
It is `T-102` — and it is **DISCHARGED**, not open. `C-0071` (iteration 14) found it had stopped applying: it was raised by the flexure-and-tie branch that `CH-0081` removed from the output role, and **nobody noticed at the time**. Kept here with its analysis intact, struck rather than deleted, because *a list that only ever grows is not a record* — the same discipline [`DECISIONS-FOR-NDI.md`](DECISIONS-FOR-NDI.md) applies in its discharged table. **Do not carry it to NDI.**

**8. Which device does §3's *desired* clause ask for — a 33.3 pN/nm coupling travelling 10 nm, or a 10 pN/nm one?**
**ANSWERED, 2026-08-18 — TWO DEVICES.**
So `δ ≤ F/|k_eff|` = 3.58–4.27 nm bounds **device A**, and device B is the 10 pN/nm placement, refused by
`C-0017`'s stability floor at **2.34–2.79×** — *at the 10 nm layer in 2 mM*, which is the state items 3 and 9
together say device B need not occupy. **`T-192` has run and that state is not an escape either: `C-0110`
admits device B at 1 of 96 tall states and refuses device A at 96 of 96.**
Raised by `C-0046` (`T-99`) as `CH-0059`, and it is the third **specification** question this branch has produced.
`C-0017` establishes that placement is arithmetic: `k_c = F/Δs`.
Every claim in the corpus has evaluated §3's *desired* 10 nm stroke on the coupling placed for its *acceptable*
3 nm one — `k_c = 33.3333 pN/nm` — which at full stroke must deliver **333 pN**, 3.3× what §3 asks for;
`C-0039` finds the real element needs **699 pN** there and reports it as *"seven times §3's own 100 pN"*.
The desired clause's own coupling is `k_c = 100 pN / 10 nm = **10 pN/nm**`, a **different device**.
`C-0046` builds it: arms of 11.4–18.1 nm that reach 10 nm inside `C-0023`'s compliance ceiling and inside the
10 pN unzip allowable, at **12 of 29** points.
**And `C-0017`'s stability floor refuses every one of them** — `|k_eff| = 23.41–27.91 pN/nm` at the 10 nm layer
in 2 mM, so a 10 pN/nm coupling is **2.34–2.79× below the floor**.
**The threshold, and it names no element at all:** composing the two clauses gives `δ ≤ F/|k_eff|`, i.e.
**3.58–4.27 nm at §3's 100 pN**.
So the answer decides *where* the desired stroke fails, not whether — and it is worth the 34-path floor that
three claims quote as though it were a property of the material.
It is `T-112`.

**9. May the polymer layer be TALLER than §3's 10 nm — 17 to 26 nm?**
**ANSWERED, 2026-08-18 — NOT EXAMINED, AND IT IS THE SAME RESERVE AS ITEM 3.**
*"Beyond the regime I've bothered to examine as the debye length of operation in 2 mM MgCl2 is only about
4 nm … an interesting regime we've been reserving, again, for low MgCl2 concentrations we'd buy with
additional work on stabilizing DNA origami at low salt."*
The objection is a real one this programme had never answered: **no claim had evaluated the bias that
delivers §3's 100 pN across a 17–26 nm gap.** `T-192` has now run — **`C-0110`, and the objection is
UPHELD.** §3's 100 pN stops arriving across a gap of **13.6989 nm at 0.5 mM**, 11.8724 at 1 mM and
10.1299 at 2 mM, so a 17–26 nm layer reaches it at **0 of 12** (gap, buffer) cells; device B is admitted at
**1 of 96** states in **1 of 6** layer models, and §3's *acceptable* clause at **0 of 96**.
**The tall layer loses both devices.** What it does buy is the kinematics and only those — the *uncoupled*
tile reaches a 10 nm stroke at **52 of 96** tall states (`CH-0127`) — and it breaks §3's own effort-point
row by 1.08–1.64× before any field is solved (`CH-0126`).
Raised by `C-0050` (`T-108`), and it is the **fourth** specification question this programme has produced —
and, unlike items 6, 7 and 8, it is the only one that can buy §3's *desired* stroke at all.
The stroke is the layer's own compression, `s = L₀ − h`, so **`s < L₀` identically**:
on §3's stated heights of 5, 7 and 10 nm a ~10 nm stroke is the statement that the polymer is crushed to zero
thickness, and the actuator refuses it **1.02×** on the dry-thickness floor, **1.12×** on `C-0002`'s own
validity range and **1.35×** under §3's own 100 pN — all three **before any coupling exists**.
No output coupling can move this, because `C-0017`'s own theorem makes the delivered stroke monotone
*decreasing* in the coupling stiffness.
**The threshold:** the layer height at which the 100 pN dead-load stroke reaches 10 nm is **16.63–26.12 nm**
across `C-0003`'s six models at `σ` = 0.024 nm⁻².
~~§3's own tile row already allows the effort point to sit *"~20–25 nm above the electrode"*, so the geometry is
not absurd~~ — **withdrawn by `CH-0126`: the effort point of a 17–26 nm layer is 32–41 nm, and 27–36 nm even
with the lever bonded straight onto the tile, so §3's row refuses it rather than permitting it** —
but a 17–26 nm layer is a **different device**: `C-0002`'s concentrated crossover, `C-0005`'s
screening validity, `C-0007`'s drainage corner and `C-0012`'s bias all move with it, and nothing in this
programme has evaluated one.
**Items 6, 7 and 8 cannot substitute for it**: a bigger tile, a perforated superstructure and a softer
placement each unblock something the *coupling* needs, and none of them is a layer height.
It is `T-115`.

**10. What is the scaffold — linear or circular, M13 or synthetic, and how long?**
**ANSWERED, 2026-08-18 — CIRCULAR M13, ~7–8 k nt**, 50 k available, hierarchical assembly above that, and
*"to use exess scaffold, just make the tile thicker. The 1700 nucleotide structure the agent is proposing
seems… thin and low stiffness."*
So the **circular-with-remainder** row is the default (`T-195`), the purpose-length recommendation is declined,
and the criticism is corroborated by this repository's own sources (`C-0072`).
**The remedy names a tile this programme has never modelled and §3 has always specified** — ~10 nm thick,
four layers, which is what M13's 4.31× excess pays for. `T-191`, and it is the largest consequence of the reply.
Raised by `C-0086` (`T-151`) in iteration 17, and it is the **fifth** live specification question.
It looked like an implementation detail until `T-151` proved it is not:
**a seam is a PARITY ON A TREE, not a fabrication convention.**
Crossovers join only *adjacent* duplexes, so the row-adjacency graph of a single-layer sheet is a **path** —
a tree — and a closed walk on a tree traverses every edge an even number of times,
so a **fully folded circular** scaffold gives every row two segments and therefore exactly one seam.
Brute-forced: the path graph carries **2 Hamiltonian paths and ZERO Hamiltonian cycles** at every width 3–12.
**A seam needs both premises and the Gen-1 tile fails the second anyway** — the sheet takes **1 680** of
M13's **7 249** nt.
**The cost of guessing wrong:** `C-0081` shows a seam deletes a weave extremum, putting **6–12 of `C-0063`'s
34** arm stations off the node, and the parity that *closes* takes the worst across-row clearance to
**0.122724 nm** — and to **−0.002276 nm** at the measured phosphate girth, a clash.
**The bracket:** linear costs Rothemund's own measured yield, **63 %** well-formed circular against **11 %**
on the BsrBI-linearised scaffold; circular-and-fully-folded costs the seam; circular-with-remainder costs a
**5 569 nt, 33.3 nm** unpaired coil carrying **1.66×** the sheet's own charge in the actuated gap.
A purpose-built **1 680 nt** circular scaffold removes all three and needs a **67 nt** return loop.
**And it quantises the tile WIDTH**: a seamless raster admits only odd multiples of 16 bp — 16, 48, 80, 112,
144 — so **§3's 40.0 nm = 117.6 bp is not a buildable seamless width**, the nearest being **112 bp = 38.08 nm**
(`CH-0101`, `T-153`).
It is `T-154`, and it is item 5 of [`DECISIONS-FOR-NDI.md`](DECISIONS-FOR-NDI.md).

**11. Does §3 admit a TWO-LAYER tile, or is single-layer a requirement?**
Raised by `C-0093` (`T-166`) in iteration 19 as the **sixth** specification question, and recorded here for the
first time on 2026-08-18 — **it lived in [`DECISIONS-FOR-NDI.md`](DECISIONS-FOR-NDI.md) and never in this
list**, which is `C-0071`'s *"a discharge is invisible to the queue"* running the other way: an **addition**
the register never learned about, found only because the answers arrived and there were six of them.
**ANSWERED BY IMPLICATION, 2026-08-18 — YES, and volunteered rather than granted**: *"to use exess scaffold,
just make the tile thicker."*
The question asked whether a second **body** may be tied to the tile at many out-of-plane sites; what was
answered is that the **tile itself** may be thicker, which is the stronger permission — `C-0093`'s shared
stiff body **fused** to the tile rather than tied to it, which deletes the ties and their compliance.
Recorded as **inferred**, and it is the one answer worth confirming in a sentence. `T-191`.
